### Q1: The heart is:

- **A)** Ectodermal in origin.
- **B)** Endodermal in origin.
- **C)** Splanchnic mesodermal in origin.
- **D)** Mesodermal in origin.
- **E)** Endo-Mesodermal in origin.

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q2: The heart is formed infront of:

- **A)** Buccopharyngeal membrane from somatic mesoderm.
- **B)** Buccopharyngeal membrane from splanchnic mesoderm.
- **C)** Cloacal membrane.
- **D)** Buccopharyngeal membrane from Paraxial mesoderm.
- **E)** Septum transversum.

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q3: The visceral layer of pericardium is derived from:

- **D)** Paraxial mesoderm.
- **E)** Extraembryonic mesoderm.

**Correct Answer:** B

# Source File: 02_Dept_Biochem_2026.md

### Q1: Regulation of Cholesterol Synthesis

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Key regulatory enzyme is HMG-CoA reductase. Regulated by: 1) Feedback inhibition by intracellular cholesterol; 2) Hormonal regulation: Insulin and thyroid hormones activate (dephosphorylation) while glucagon and epinephrine inactivate (phosphorylation by AMP-activated protein kinase); 3) Transcriptional regulation via SREBP-2 (Sterol Regulatory Element-Binding Protein); 4) Competitive inhibition by statins.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q2: Factors that Increase Plasma Cholesterol

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) High dietary saturated fats and cholesterol; 2) Hypothyroidism (decreased LDL receptor expression); 3) Diabetes mellitus (impaired lipoprotein clearance); 4) Obstructive jaundice (presence of lipoprotein X); 5) Nephrotic syndrome; 6) Familial hypercholesterolemia (mutations in LDL receptor).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q3: Factors that Decrease Plasma Cholesterol

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Hyperthyroidism (increased clearance via upregulated LDL receptors); 2) Severe liver diseases (decreased endogenous synthesis); 3) Malnutrition/malabsorption; 4) Diet rich in polyunsaturated fatty acids (PUFA) and plant stanols; 5) Hypolipidemic drugs (Statins, Ezetimibe, Bile acid sequestrants, PCSK9 inhibitors).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q4: The metabolism of chylomicrons

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Synthesized in intestinal mucosal cells from dietary lipids (TAG, cholesterol, Apo B-48); 2) Secreted into lymphatic system and enter blood via thoracic duct; 3) Mature by acquiring Apo C-II and Apo E from HDL; 4) Apo C-II activates lipoprotein lipase (LPL) on capillary endothelium of adipose tissue and muscle to hydrolyze TAG into glycerol and FFAs; 5) Chylomicron remnants depleted of TAG are taken up by the liver via Apo E recognition by LDL-receptor-related protein (LRP).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q5: Apoproteins functions

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Apo B-48: Structural protein for chylomicrons, required for secretion; 2) Apo B-100: Structural protein for VLDL and LDL, ligand for LDL receptor; 3) Apo C-II: Activator of lipoprotein lipase (LPL); 4) Apo E: Recognition ligand for hepatic clearance of remnants and IDL; 5) Apo A-I: Major structural apoprotein of HDL, activates LCAT (lecithin-cholesterol acyltransferase).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q6: Role of Lipoprotein (a) in heart disease

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Lipoprotein(a) consists of an LDL-like particle containing Apo B-100 covalently linked to apolipoprotein(a). Apo(a) shares high structural homology with plasminogen, competing with plasminogen for binding to endothelial cells and fibrin, thereby inhibiting fibrinolysis and promoting thrombosis. It also accelerates atherogenesis by promoting endothelial dysfunction and foam cell formation, serving as an independent risk factor for CAD and myocardial infarction.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q7: Importance of HDL

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Mediates reverse cholesterol transport, removing excess cholesterol from peripheral tissues and transporting it to the liver for biliary excretion; 2) Contains Apo A-I which activates LCAT to esterify cholesterol; 3) Acts as a circulating reservoir for Apo C-II and Apo E for chylomicrons and VLDL; 4) Has anti-inflammatory, antioxidant (via paraoxonase), and anti-thrombotic properties.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q8: Atherosclerosis and LDL (mechanism)

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Excess circulating LDL penetrates endothelial barrier into arterial intima; 2) LDL undergoes oxidative modification by free radicals; 3) Oxidized LDL induces endothelial activation, expressing adhesion molecules (VCAM-1) and chemoattractants (MCP-1) that recruit monocytes; 4) Monocytes differentiate into macrophages; 5) Macrophages ingest oxidized LDL via scavenger receptors (SR-A, CD36) forming lipid-laden foam cells; 6) Foam cells release cytokines promoting smooth muscle migration and fibrous plaque formation.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q9: Risk factors for atherosclerosis

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Major modifiable: Dyslipidemia (high LDL, low HDL), hypertension, cigarette smoking, diabetes mellitus; 2) Non-modifiable: Advanced age, male sex, family history of premature CAD, genetic polymorphisms; 3) Other/Emerging: High CRP, hyperhomocysteinemia, elevated Lipoprotein(a), obesity, physical inactivity.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q10: Prevention of atherosclerosis

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Lifestyle modifications: Healthy diet low in saturated fats and trans fats, high in soluble fiber and omega-3 fatty acids; 2) Regular aerobic exercise and weight management; 3) Smoking cessation; 4) Strict glycemic control in diabetics and BP control (<130/80 mmHg); 5) Pharmacological therapy: Statins for dyslipidemia, low-dose aspirin in high-risk patients.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q11: All of the following are true regarding risk factors for myocardial infarction EXCEPT:

- **A)** Omega-3 fatty acids decrease LDL
- **B)** Cigarette smoking is an independent risk factor for MI
- **C)** C-reactive protein is an independent risk factor for MI
- **D)** Elevated HDL is a risk factor for MI

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q12: Atheromatous changes of blood vessels affect early in:

- **A)** Kidney
- **B)** Heart (coronary arteries and abdominal aorta)
- **C)** Liver
- **D)** Spleen

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q13: Atherosclerosis and arteriosclerosis are classified as diseases of:

- **A)** Arteries
- **B)** Bones
- **C)** Liver
- **D)** Kidneys

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q14: The disease in which cholesterol and fatty materials accumulate in the wall of arteries is:

- **A)** Diabetic syndrome
- **B)** Tuberculosis
- **C)** Arteriosclerosis / Atherosclerosis
- **D)** Nephrotic syndrome

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q15: What is the biosynthetic source of all steroid hormones?

- **A)** Cholesterol
- **B)** Ketone bodies
- **C)** Carbohydrate
- **D)** Protein

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q16: The lipoprotein particles that have the highest percentage concentration of cholesterol is:

**Correct Answer:** C

---

- **B)** Cholesteryl esterase
- **C)** HMG-CoA reductase
- **D)** HMG-CoA lyase

**Correct Answer:** C

- **A)** Apo E

- **C)** Apo B-48
- **D)** Apo C-II

### Q20: The triacylglycerol content of plasma lipoproteins are hydrolyzed by:

- **C)** Gastric lipase
- **D)** Intestinal lipase

- **A)** Present in the liver but not in adipose tissue
- **B)** Activated by Apo C-II
- **C)** Activated by Apo A

### Q22: All of the following cause hypercholesterolemia EXCEPT:

- **A)** Diabetes mellitus
- **B)** Hypothyroidism
- **C)** Obstructive jaundice
- **D)** Severe liver diseases

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q23: One of the following plasma lipoproteins is discoidal in shape:

- **A)** Nascent HDL
- **B)** Chylomicrons
- **C)** VLDL
- **D)** LDL

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q24: The major source of extracellular cholesterol for human peripheral tissues is:

- **A)** VLDL
- **B)** LDL
- **C)** HDL
- **D)** IDL

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q25: A patient presents for a complete blood screening after having a high dietary fat meal less than 15 minutes ago. What will you find in the blood screening?

- **A)** High level of chylomicrons (milky plasma)
- **B)** High level of HMG-CoA reductase

### Q16: Enumerate two virulence factors of Yersinia pestis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Fraction 1 (F1) capsular antigen (antiphagocytic capsule).

3. Endotoxin (LPS) and Murine exotoxin.

**Correct Answer:** -

### Q17: Mention diseases caused by Cytomegalovirus (CMV).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Congenital cytomegalic inclusion disease (microcephaly, sensorineural deafness, hepatosplenomegaly).
2. Infectious mononucleosis-like syndrome (heterophile-negative).
3. Severe opportunistic infections in immunocompromised/AIDS patients (interstitial pneumonitis, retinitis, hepatitis).

**Correct Answer:** -

### Q18: Mention tumours caused by Epstein-Barr virus (EBV).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Burkitt lymphoma (especially African jaw type).
2. Nasopharyngeal carcinoma.
3. Hodgkin and Non-Hodgkin lymphomas.
4. Post-transplant lymphoproliferative disorder (PTLD).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q19: Enumerate two reservoirs (sources) of Yersinia pestis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Wild rodents (rats, mice, prairie dogs) - main animal reservoir.
2. Rodent fleas (Xenopsylla cheopis) - insect vector.
3. Infected humans with pneumonic plague (direct droplet transmission).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q20: Enumerate two modes of transmission of Malta fever (Brucellosis).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Ingestion of raw/unpasteurized dairy products (milk, cheese).
2. Direct contact of broken skin or mucous membranes with infected animal tissues, blood, or placenta.
3. Inhalation of infected aerosols in slaughterhouses or laboratories.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q21: Enumerate two methods for diagnosis of viral myocarditis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Isolation of virus from myocardial biopsy or throat/stool samples on tissue culture.
2. Molecular detection of viral RNA/DNA by RT-PCR.
3. Serological detection of specific IgM or a four-fold rise in IgG titer by ELISA.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q22: Mention preventive measures for Rheumatic fever.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Primary prevention: Prompt and complete treatment of Streptococcus pyogenes pharyngitis with penicillin for a full 10-day course.
2. Secondary prevention: Long-acting benzathine penicillin G monthly injections to prevent recurrence in patients with prior rheumatic fever.
3. Antibiotic prophylaxis before dental procedures for patients with established rheumatic heart disease.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q23: Mention preventive measures for Subacute Bacterial Endocarditis (SBE).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Prophylactic administration of oral amoxicillin (or clindamycin/azithromycin in penicillin-allergic patients) 30-60 minutes before dental or oral surgical procedures involving gingival manipulation.
2. Good oral and dental hygiene.
3. Secondary antibiotic prophylaxis in patients with pre-existing valvular disease.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q24: What is the most reliable method for prevention and control of plague?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Vector and reservoir control measures: Application of insecticides (anti-flea measures) followed by rodent control (anti-rat measures), combined with quarantine of pneumonic cases and chemoprophylaxis with doxycycline for close contacts.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q25: Give reason: Long-acting penicillin is given monthly after rheumatic carditis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
To prevent recurrent Group A streptococcal pharyngeal infections, which would trigger secondary immune reactions, reactivation of rheumatic fever, and progressive permanent destruction of cardiac valves.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q26: Give reason: Antibiotic prophylaxis is required before oral surgery in patients with a history of rheumatic fever.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Dental manipulations produce transient bacteremia with oral viridans streptococci (Streptococcus mutans, mitis). In patients with rheumatic valve damage, these bacteria readily adhere to the abnormal endothelial surface and fibrin deposits, causing Subacute Bacterial Endocarditis (SBE).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q27: Give reason: Blood culture has no role in the diagnosis of rheumatic fever.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Rheumatic fever is not a direct bacterial infection of the heart, but a sterile post-streptococcal autoimmune disease caused by cross-reactivity (molecular mimicry) between streptococcal M protein and host cardiac tissues. The bacterium has usually been cleared by the time rheumatic symptoms appear.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q28: Give reason: Rat and flea control measures are critical in plague prevention.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Because wild and domestic rodents (rats) are the natural animal reservoir and rat fleas (Xenopsylla cheopis) are the primary transmitting vector of Yersinia pestis to humans. Insecticides must precede rodenticides to prevent starved fleas from abandoning dead rats and biting humans.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q29: Give reason: A pregnant female suffering from infectious mononucleosis-like disease in the 1st trimester needs close observation.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
The first trimester is the critical period of fetal organogenesis. If the mononucleosis-like illness is caused by Cytomegalovirus (CMV) or Toxoplasma gondii, the pathogen can cross the placenta and cause severe congenital cytomegalic inclusion disease, microcephaly, sensorineural deafness, chorioretinitis, or miscarriage.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q30: Classify bacterial endocarditis regarding the causative organism and clinical course.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Acute Bacterial Endocarditis (ABE): Rapid, fulminant destruction of normal or abnormal valves, caused predominantly by virulent bacteria like Staphylococcus aureus and Streptococcus pyogenes.
2. Subacute Bacterial Endocarditis (SBE): Indolent, protracted course affecting previously damaged or abnormal valves, caused predominantly by low-virulence organisms like Streptococcus viridans and Enterococcus faecalis.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q31: Classify Coxsackievirus regarding inoculation into suckling mice.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Coxsackievirus Group A: Causes diffuse myositis of skeletal muscles leading to generalized flaccid paralysis, with no prominent lesions in the CNS or viscera.
2. Coxsackievirus Group B: Causes focal myositis, patchy skeletal muscle lesions, degeneration of brown fat, necrotizing hepatitis, pancreatitis, myocarditis, and spastic paralysis.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q32: Classify Yersinia species according to clinical diseases and sources.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Yersinia pestis: Causes plague (bubonic, septicemic, pneumonic); transmitted by rat flea bites or respiratory droplets from rodents/humans.
2. Yersinia enterocolitica & Yersinia pseudotuberculosis: Cause gastroenteritis, mesenteric lymphadenitis (pseudoappendicitis), and reactive arthritis; transmitted via ingestion of contaminated food and water.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q33: Compare between Streptococcus pyogenes and Streptococcus viridans regarding carditis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Streptococcus pyogenes: Causes acute pancarditis (endocarditis, myocarditis with Aschoff bodies, pericarditis) as a post-infectious sterile autoimmune sequela (rheumatic fever); blood cultures are negative for the organism in the heart.
2. Streptococcus viridans: Directly colonizes damaged endocardium/valves forming infective bacterial vegetations (Subacute Bacterial Endocarditis); blood cultures are positive and viable bacteria are present on the valve.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q34: Compare between Cytomegalovirus (CMV) and Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) regarding serious disease.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Cytomegalovirus (CMV): Causes severe congenital anomalies (microcephaly, sensorineural hearing loss, hepatosplenomegaly, mental retardation) and severe opportunistic infections in AIDS/transplant patients (retinitis, interstitial pneumonitis).
2. Epstein-Barr virus (EBV): Causes oncogenic malignancies (Burkitt lymphoma, nasopharyngeal carcinoma, Hodgkin lymphoma) and oral hairy leukoplakia.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q35: Compare between Cytomegalovirus (CMV) and Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) regarding mode of transmission.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. CMV: Transmitted transplacentally (vertical), perinatally through birth canal and breast milk, via saliva, sexual contact, blood transfusions, and organ transplantation.
2. EBV: Transmitted primarily by infected saliva ('kissing disease') via close oral contact, and less commonly via blood transfusion or bone marrow transplant.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q36: Compare between Brucella and Yersinia regarding mode of transmission.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Brucella: Transmitted via ingestion of unpasteurized milk and dairy products, direct contact with tissues/fluids of infected livestock (sheep, goats, cattle), or inhalation of infective aerosols.
2. Yersinia: Transmitted via the bite of infected rat fleas (Xenopsylla cheopis), inhalation of respiratory droplets from patients with pneumonic plague, or ingestion of contaminated food/water (Y. enterocolitica).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q37: Compare between Brucella and Yersinia regarding one method of prevention.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. Brucella: Pasteurization of all milk and dairy products, vaccination of cattle/goats, and wearing protective equipment when handling livestock.
2. Yersinia: Vector control using insecticides against fleas, rodent eradication, strict quarantine of pneumonic cases, and prophylactic doxycycline for contacts.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q38: Compare between Cytomegalovirus (CMV) and Coxsackievirus regarding mode of transmission.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. CMV: Transmitted vertically (transplacental), via breast milk, saliva, sexual contact, blood transfusions, and organ transplants.
2. Coxsackievirus: Transmitted predominantly via the fecal-oral route (ingestion of contaminated food/water) and through aerosolized respiratory droplets.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q39: True or False: Toxic shock syndrome can be caused by Streptococcus pyogenes.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
True (Streptococcal Toxic Shock Syndrome is mediated by Streptococcal pyrogenic exotoxins SpeA, SpeC which act as superantigens).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q40: True or False: Subacute bacterial endocarditis is mainly caused by viridans streptococci while acute bacterial endocarditis is more likely due to Staphylococcus aureus.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
True (Viridans streptococci cause indolent subacute endocarditis on abnormal valves, while Staphylococcus aureus causes destructive acute endocarditis even on normal valves).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q41: Why is antibiotic prophylaxis given before dental and oral surgical procedures?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
To prevent Subacute Bacterial Endocarditis (SBE) caused by oral viridans streptococci entering the bloodstream during gingival manipulation and colonizing abnormal or prosthetic heart valves.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q42: Why do AIDS patients commonly suffer from repeated opportunistic fungal infections?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Fungi are opportunistic pathogens held in check by cellular immunity. Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV) selectively infects and destroys CD4+ T-helper lymphocytes, severely depleting cell-mediated immunity and leaving the patient highly susceptible to fungal infections like Candida albicans, Cryptococcus neoformans, and Pneumocystis jirovecii.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q43: Compare between EBV and CMV regarding mode of transmission and one primary disease produced by each.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1. EBV: Transmitted via infected saliva (kissing disease); produces infectious mononucleosis (heterophile antibody-positive) and Burkitt lymphoma.
2. CMV: Transmitted transplacentally, via breast milk, saliva, and sexual contact; produces congenital cytomegalic inclusion disease and heterophile-negative mononucleosis-like syndrome.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q44: Give reason: Rheumatic fever occurs 2 to 3 weeks after Streptococcus pyogenes pharyngitis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
A latent period of 2 to 3 weeks is required for the patient's immune system to undergo sensitization, clonal expansion of B cells, and synthesis of high titers of antibodies against streptococcal antigens (such as M protein) that cross-react with human myocardial and valvular glycoproteins (molecular mimicry).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

- **B)** Decreased baroreceptor firing causes increased sympathetic effects

### Q28: In ventricular myocytes, phase 2 (the plateau phase) of the action potential is characterized by which one of the following?

### Q40: 67 years old, heavy smoker, obese mat, presented to the ER with acute chest pain. You suspected acute myocardial infarction and ordered an ECG. During recording the ECG, The ECG machine records a positive (+ve) wave in which of the following conditions?

### Q44: 21 years old female presented to emergency room with drowsiness, rapid pulsation and hypotension after psychic trauma. What is the first compensatory mechanism takes place to regulate her arterial blood pressure?

### Q45: Regarding coronary blood flow, which of the following isn't true?

### Q50: In a laboratory experiment, a drug that only activates the SA node was given to an animal to increase its heart rate. ### Q61: A 24-years old healthy individual that has been exercising for more than one hour, his cardiac output was measured at different times from the beginning of exercise, and all measurements revealed an increase in the cardiac output. - **B)** Increases coronary blood flow by causing vasodilation indirectly

- **D)** Increases coronary blood flow by increasing blood viscosity

- **B)** open Ca++ channels, causing increased Ca++ entry and a stronger contraction

### Q35: Explain the effect of increased venous return on the heart rate (Bainbridge reflex).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
An increase in venous return increases the volume of blood entering the right atrium, stretching low-pressure stretch receptors (type B atrial receptors) located at the veno-atrial junctions. Afferent impulses travel via the vagus nerve to the medulla (nucleus tractus solitarius), which reflexively increases sympathetic efferent discharge to the sinoatrial (SA) node and decreases parasympathetic tone. This results in reflex tachycardia (Bainbridge reflex), preventing venous pooling and matching cardiac output to venous return.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q36: Discuss the mechanism of blood pressure regulation by the renin-angiotensin-aldosterone system (RAAS).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Trigger: Decreased renal perfusion pressure (sensed by renal baroreceptors in afferent arterioles), decreased Na+ delivery to macula densa, or increased renal sympathetic activity stimulates juxtaglomerular (JG) cells to secrete renin into the bloodstream; 2) Enzymatic Cascade: Renin cleaves circulating angiotensinogen (from liver) to form angiotensin I (decapeptide). Angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE), predominantly on pulmonary endothelial cells, cleaves Ang I to form Angiotensin II (octapeptide); 3) Actions of Angiotensin II: Potent direct arteriolar vasoconstriction (via AT1 receptors) increasing Total Peripheral Resistance (TPR); stimulates adrenal cortex (zona glomerulosa) to secrete aldosterone, promoting renal tubular Na+ and water reabsorption and K+ excretion; stimulates pituitary secretion of vasopressin (ADH); stimulates thirst center in hypothalamus; increases sympathetic outflow.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q37: Explain on a biochemical basis how vegetable oils and Polyunsaturated Fatty Acids (PUFA) protect against atherosclerosis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Polyunsaturated fatty acids (PUFAs, such as linoleic acid and omega-3 alpha-linolenic, EPA, and DHA) protect against atherosclerosis through multiple biochemical mechanisms: 1) Upregulation of hepatic LDL receptors: PUFAs increase LDL receptor gene expression, enhancing the clearance of atherogenic LDL from circulation and lowering plasma LDL-cholesterol; 2) Promotion of cholesterol excretion: PUFAs enhance hepatic 7-alpha-hydroxylase activity, accelerating the conversion of cholesterol into bile acids for fecal elimination; 3) Reduction of hepatic VLDL secretion: Omega-3 PUFAs reduce hepatic synthesis and secretion of triacylglycerol and VLDL by inhibiting diacylglycerol acyltransferase (DGAT) and promoting fatty acid beta-oxidation; 4) Anti-inflammatory and antithrombotic effects: Omega-3 PUFAs compete with arachidonic acid, shifting eicosanoid synthesis toward less inflammatory series-3 prostaglandins and series-5 leukotrienes, and anti-aggregatory thromboxane A3, suppressing platelet aggregation and endothelial inflammation.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q32: Describe the course, beginning, and termination of the Superior Vena Cava (SVC).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Begins behind the lower border of the right 1st costal cartilage by the confluence of right and left brachiocephalic veins. Descends vertically behind the right border of sternum in superior and middle mediastinum, piercing the fibrous pericardium at the level of 2nd costal cartilage. Terminates by opening into the upper part of the right atrium behind the right 3rd costal cartilage. Its major tributary is the azygos vein.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q33: What is the surgical importance of the transverse pericardial sinus?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
The transverse sinus lies posterior to the ascending aorta and pulmonary trunk, and anterior to the SVC and left atrium. During cardiac surgery (e.g. coronary artery bypass or aortic repair), a ligature or surgical clamp can be passed through the transverse sinus to temporarily occlude both the aorta and pulmonary trunk, halting blood flow while establishing cardiopulmonary bypass.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Beginning: Formed by the union of the medial end of the dorsal venous arch of foot with the medial marginal vein, passing anterior to the medial malleolus. 2) Course & Termination: Ascends on the medial side of the leg and thigh, passes through the saphenous opening, and terminates in the femoral vein ~3.5 cm below and lateral to the pubic tubercle. 3) Surgical Importance: Commonly harvested as a vascular graft for coronary artery bypass grafting (CABG); site for emergency saphenous cutdown; prone to varicosities.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q35: Enumerate three anatomical variations of the coronary arteries.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Left coronary dominance: Posterior interventricular artery arises from the circumflex branch of the left coronary artery instead of the RCA (occurs in ~10-15%). 2) Co-dominance: Branches from both RCA and circumflex LCA run in or near the posterior interventricular groove. 3) Origin variation: Single coronary artery arising from a single aortic sinus, or circumflex artery arising aberrantly from the right coronary sinus.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q36: Define sudden cardiac death (SCD).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Sudden cardiac death is unexpected death from cardiac causes occurring within a short time period (usually within 1 hour of symptom onset) in a person with or without pre-existing cardiac disease. Most commonly caused by lethal ventricular tachyarrhythmias (ventricular fibrillation/ventricular tachycardia) secondary to ischemic heart disease / acute coronary syndromes.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q37: Define cardiomyopathy.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Cardiomyopathy is a heterogeneous group of myocardial diseases associated with mechanical and/or electrical dysfunction, exhibiting inappropriate ventricular hypertrophy or dilatation. Classified primarily into dilated cardiomyopathy (DCM), hypertrophic cardiomyopathy (HCM), and restrictive cardiomyopathy (RCM).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q38: Enumerate four granulomatous diseases affecting the myocardium.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Tuberculosis (myocardial TB); 2) Sarcoidosis (cardiac sarcoidosis); 3) Rheumatic heart disease (Aschoff bodies / granulomas); 4) Giant cell myocarditis; 5) Fungal myocarditis (e.g. Histoplasmosis).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q39: Enumerate the major and minor Jones criteria for the diagnosis of acute rheumatic fever.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Major criteria (JONES): Joint involvement (migratory polyarthritis), O/Carditis, Nodules (subcutaneous), Erythema marginatum, Sydenham chorea. 2) Minor criteria: Clinical: fever, arthralgia; Laboratory: elevated acute phase reactants (ESR, CRP), prolonged PR interval on ECG. Diagnosis requires 2 major OR 1 major + 2 minor criteria plus evidence of preceding streptococcal infection (elevated ASO or positive throat culture).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q40: Enumerate three biological functions of cholesterol.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Essential structural component of cellular plasma membranes, modulating membrane fluidity and permeability; 2) Precursor for the biosynthesis of all steroid hormones (glucocorticoids, mineralocorticoids, androgens, estrogens, progesterone); 3) Precursor for bile acid synthesis (cholic and chenodeoxycholic acids) in the liver required for dietary fat digestion; 4) Precursor for vitamin D3 (cholecalciferol) synthesis in the skin upon UV exposure.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q41: Explain why Lipoprotein(a) has a significant role in coronary heart disease.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Lipoprotein(a) [Lp(a)] consists of an LDL particle linked to apolipoprotein(a). Apo(a) has structural homology to plasminogen but lacks fibrinolytic activity. It competitively inhibits plasminogen binding to fibrin and endothelial surfaces, impairing plasmin formation and fibrinolysis, thereby promoting atherothrombosis. Additionally, Lp(a) undergoes oxidative modification, facilitating macrophage foam cell formation within arterial intima.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q42: Describe the light microscopic structure of tunica intima in muscular arteries.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Tunica intima consists of: 1) Endothelium: single continuous layer of flattened, polygonal simple squamous cells resting on a basal lamina; 2) Subendothelial connective tissue: loose CT containing collagen fibers, fine elastic fibers, and scattered smooth muscle cells/myointimal cells; 3) Internal elastic lamina (IEL): a prominent, well-developed, fenestrated wavy band of elastin that demarcates the boundary between the tunica intima and tunica media.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q43: Describe the light microscopic structure of cardiac muscle fibers.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Striated involuntary muscle fibers consisting of cylindrical branching and anastomosing cells (cardiomyocytes); 2) Each cell typically contains a single (occasionally binucleated) centrally located oval euchromatic nucleus; 3) Sarcoplasm contains abundant mitochondria, glycogen granules, and perinuclear lipofuscin pigment in aging cells; 4) Cells are joined end-to-end by specialized junctional complexes called intercalated discs containing fascia adherens, macula adherens (desmosomes), and gap junctions.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q44: Enumerate three cardiovascular reflexes affecting heart rate.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Arterial baroreceptor reflex: Carotid sinus and aortic arch baroreceptors respond to increased arterial pressure by increasing vagal parasympathetic output to SA node, slowing HR (bradycardia), and decreasing sympathetic activity; 2) Bainbridge (atrial) reflex: Stretch of right atrial wall by increased venous return stimulates low-pressure stretch receptors, reflexively increasing HR via sympathetic efferents to prevent atrial blood pooling; 3) Peripheral chemoreceptor reflex: Hypoxia/hypercapnia stimulates carotid and aortic bodies, producing reflex tachycardia and increased vasoconstriction.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q45: Explain the physiological importance of the long absolute refractory period (ARP) in cardiac muscle.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Cardiac muscle action potential exhibits a prolonged plateau phase (phase 2) mediated by inward L-type Ca2+ current, giving an ARP that lasts almost as long as the entire mechanical contraction (~250-300 ms). Importance: 1) Prevents summation and tetanic contractions, ensuring the heart alternates rhythmically between contraction (systole) and relaxation (diastole) for ventricular filling; 2) Prevents premature re-excitation and re-entrant tachyarrhythmias, maintaining unidirectional electrical wave propagation.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q11: Enumerate the causes of the pacemaker potential (prepotential) in the SA node.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
The pacemaker potential (prepotential) in the SA node is caused by three ionic mechanisms: 1) Decreasing potassium efflux: progressive inactivation of delayed rectifier K+ channels decreases outward hyperpolarizing K+ current; 2) Funny current (If): inward sodium current through hyperpolarization-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated (HCN) channels activated at negative potentials below -50 mV; 3) Calcium influx: transient opening of low-voltage-activated T-type Ca2+ channels in the late phase of prepotential, followed by L-type Ca2+ channel opening at threshold (-40 mV) to generate the upstroke.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q12: Explain the mechanisms by which moderate hypoxia increases heart rate.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Moderate hypoxia increases heart rate through: 1) Peripheral chemoreceptor stimulation: carotid and aortic body chemoreceptors sense reduced arterial PO2, stimulating the medullary cardiac center to increase sympathetic efferent discharge to the SA node; 2) Increased circulating catecholamines: hypoxia stimulates the adrenal medulla to release epinephrine and norepinephrine; 3) Pulmonary stretch receptor reflex: hypoxia induces hyperventilation, and lung inflation stimulates vagal pulmonary stretch receptors which reflexively inhibit the cardiac vagal center, causing tachycardia.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q13: Enumerate the limits and components of cardiac reserve.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Cardiac reserve is the difference between the maximum cardiac output an individual can achieve and the cardiac output at rest. Factors/limits determining cardiac reserve include: 1) Heart rate reserve: maximal HR (approx. 220 - age) versus resting HR (60-80 bpm); 2) Stroke volume reserve: ability to increase SV from resting (~70 mL) to maximal (~120-150 mL in athletes) via increased end-diastolic volume (Frank-Starling) and increased myocardial contractility (inotropic reserve); 3) Coronary flow reserve: maximal increase in coronary blood flow during maximal exertion (normally 4-5 fold above baseline).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Anterior: Manubrium sterni; 2) Posterior: Bodies of the upper four thoracic vertebrae (T1-T4); 3) Superior: Superior thoracic aperture (plane of thoracic inlet, formed by upper border of manubrium, 1st rib, and body of T1); 4) Inferior: Transverse thoracic plane (imaginary horizontal line passing from sternal angle anteriorly to lower border of T4 vertebra posteriorly); 5) Lateral: Mediastinal pleura of the lungs on both sides.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Involuntary striated muscle consisting of cylindrical branching and anastomosing cells; 2) Each cardiomyocyte has a single centrally located oval euchromatic nucleus (occasionally binucleated); 3) Sarcoplasm contains cross-striations with A and I bands, rich in mitochondria and glycogen; 4) Cardiomyocytes are joined end-to-end by intercalated discs with transverse steps (fascia adherens and desmosomes for mechanical anchoring) and longitudinal segments (gap junctions for electrical coupling); 5) Endomysium surrounding each fiber contains a rich capillary plexus.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Myocarditis in hand, foot, and mouth disease: Coxsackievirus A16 (or Enterovirus 71 / EV-A71). 2) Myocarditis in undulant fever: Brucella species (most commonly Brucella melitensis or Brucella abortus).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q17: Define the following: 1) Rheumatic fever; 2) Angina pectoris; 3) Cardiomyopathy; 4) Myocarditis.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) Rheumatic fever: An acute, immunologically mediated, multi-system inflammatory disease occurring 2-3 weeks after Group A beta-hemolytic streptococcal (Streptococcus pyogenes) pharyngeal infection, characterized by cross-reactive antibodies attacking host cardiac and other tissues. 2) Angina pectoris: A clinical syndrome characterized by paroxysmal, transient substernal chest discomfort or pressure, radiating to left shoulder/arm or jaw, precipitated by myocardial ischemia due to imbalance between myocardial oxygen supply and demand, without causing myocyte necrosis. 3) Cardiomyopathy: A heterogeneous group of myocardial diseases associated with mechanical and/or electrical dysfunction, typically presenting with inappropriate ventricular hypertrophy or dilatation, not secondary to CAD, hypertension, or valvular disease. 4) Myocarditis: Inflammatory disease of the myocardium diagnosed by histological, immunological, and immunohistochemical criteria, most commonly caused by viral infection (Coxsackievirus B, adenovirus, parvovirus B19).

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q18: Mention four gross features of myocardium in acute myocardial infarction (24 hours to 7 days).

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
1) 12 to 24 hours: Dark mottling / pale cyanotic discoloration; 2) 24 to 72 hours: Mottling with yellow-tan infarct center surrounded by a hyperemic border; 3) 3 to 7 days: Hyperemic border becomes more distinct with central soft yellow-tan softening (maximal risk of myocardial rupture); 4) 7 to 10 days: Maximally soft yellow-tan center with depressed red-tan margins of early granulation tissue formation.

**Correct Answer:** -

epithelium (mesothelium), contains coronary blood vessels, nerves, and adipose
tissue.

### Q9: Discuss the types of cardiac muscle fibers.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
a. Contractile:
i.
Atrial: smaller in size, fewer T-tubules.
ii.
Ventricular: larger in size (making contraction stronger), more T-tubules.
b. Conducting: fewer myofibrils and lacking typical intercalated discs.
i.
Nodal cell (initiative and conductive): smaller in size.
ii.
Purkinje fibers (conductive only): larger in diameter, paler in color, and
vacuolated.
c. Endocrine: fewer myofibrils and many electron-dense granules of ANP.

**Correct Answer:** -

Binucleated.

### Q14: Classify arteries and describe the structure of elastic arteries.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
a. Arteries:
i.
Large (elastic) arteries.
ii.
Medium-sized (muscular) arteries.
Special: coronary, cerebral, and umbilical arteries.
iii.
Small arteries (arterioles).
b. Elastic arteries:
i.
Tunica intima (10%): thick and non-folded, containingsubendothelial CT
rich in elastic fibers.
ii.
Tunica media (70%): 40-70 laminae with ↑ elastic fibers and ↓ smooth
muscles → IEL and EEL aren’t prominent.
iii.
Tunica adventitia (20%):loose CT rich in elasticfiberswith vasa vasorum
and nervi vasorum.

### Q15: Write notes on vasa vasorum and nervi vasorum.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Both are present in the tunica adventitia (in veins, elastic arteries, and larger muscular
arteries):
a. Vasa vasorum: provide blood supply to the outer half of the tunica media.
b. Nervi vasorum: vasomotor autonomic nerves.

**Correct Answer:** -

### Q16: Discuss the structural organization of connective tissue in cardiac muscle.

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
a. Endomysium: a network of reticular fibers surrounding each muscle fiber.

b. Perimysium: highly vascularized, formed of collagen and elastic fibers, present
between the bundles of cardiac muscle fibers.
c. No epimysium (instead, epicardium).
d. Purkinje fibers are grouped into bundles and surrounded by CT sheath.

Conductive cardiomyocytes present at right and left bundle branches of His and terminal

### Q1: Rheumatic Fever usually follows an infection with:

- **A)** Streptococcus group A
- **B)** Staphylococcus aureus
- **C)** Streptococcus viridans
- **D)** H.influenza

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q2: One of the tests that is done to for diagnosis of R.F:

- **A)** Alpha 1 antitrypsin
- **B)** Rheumatoid factor
- **C)** Anti-streptolysin O
- **D)** Creatine kinase

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q3: To diagnose a patient with R.F, The patient should have at least:

- **A)** One major, two minor
- **B)** Two major, one minor
- **C)** One major, one minor
- **D)** Two major, two minor

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q4: Scarlet fever is caused by:

- **A)** Staphylococcus aureus
- **B)** Streptococcus viridans
- **C)** Streptococcus pyogens
- **D)** Staphylococcus epidermidis.

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q5: The following test is the most specific for diagnosis of rheumatic fever

- **A)** Throat swab
- **B)** Blood culture
- **C)** PCR
- **D)** Antitoxin detection

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q6: On Gram staining which one of the following bacteria form chains of cocci?

- **A)** Streptococci.
- **B)** Staphylococci.
- **C)** Bacillus.
- **D)** Diplococci

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q7: Acute glomerulonephritis is a non-suppurative complication that follows infection by which one of the following organisms?

- **A)** Enterococcus faecalis
- **B)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **C)** Streptococcus pneumoniae
- **D)** Streptococcus agalatctiae

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q8: Which one of the following is NOT an important characteristic of Streptococcus pyogenes?

- **B)** M protein
- **C)** Beta-hemolysin
- **D)** Polysaccharide group-specific substance

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q9: The antistreptolysin O titer is raised in infections caused by:

- **A)** Streptococcus sanguis
- **B)** Streptococcus pneumonia
- **C)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **D)** Streptococcus bovis

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q10: A 15-year-old child with previous history of rheumatic heart disease, after dental extraction he suffered from fever and illness. Blood cultures grew show gram-positive cocci, and alpha hemolysis on Blood agar. Which one of the following is the most likely pathogen?

- **A)** Streptococcus pneumoniae
- **B)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **C)** Streptococcus agalactiae
- **D)** Streptococcus viridians

**Correct Answer:** D

---

Which one of the following is the most likely pathogen?

- **A)** Enterococcus faecium
- **B)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **C)** Streptococcus viridans
- **D)** Streptococcus mutans

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q12: A 15-year-old child with previous history of rheumatic heart disease, after dental extraction he suffered from fever and illness. Blood cultures grew show gram-positive cocci, and alpha hemolysis on Blood agar. Which one of the following is the most likely pathogen?

- **A)** Streptococcus pneumoniae
- **B)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **C)** Streptococcus agalactiae
- **D)** Streptococcus viridians

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q13: A 55-year-old man suspected to have bacterial endocarditis following GIT surgery.. Which one of the following is the most likely pathogen?

- **A)** Enterococcus faecium
- **B)** Streptococcus pyogenes
- **C)** Streptococcus viridans
- **D)** Streptococcus mutans

**Correct Answer:** A

- **A)** Bacteria and viruses in blood

- **C)** Pus and bacteria in blood
- **D)** bacteria and toxins in blood

### Q15: The following test is the most specific for diagnosis of bacteria endocarditis

- **B)** Blood culture

### Q44: Why are angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE) inhibitors not recommended during pregnancy?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
ACE inhibitors are strictly contraindicated in pregnancy (Pregnancy Category D / Black Box Warning) because they cross the placenta and disrupt fetal renal hemodynamics. This causes fetal renal tubular dysgenesis, oligohydramnios (due to decreased fetal urine output), pulmonary hypoplasia, intrauterine growth restriction (IUGR), calvarial/skull hypoplasia, and fetal death.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q45: Why might a healthcare provider choose an angiotensin II receptor blocker (ARB) over an ACE inhibitor?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
ARBs selectively block AT1 receptors without inhibiting ACE (kininase II). Because ACE also degrades bradykinin, ACE inhibitors cause bradykinin accumulation in the airways, precipitating a persistent dry cough in 10-20% of patients and angioedema in susceptible individuals. ARBs provide equivalent hemodynamic and renoprotective benefits without increasing bradykinin, making them the preferred alternative for patients experiencing ACEi-induced cough or angioedema.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q46: Why is hydralazine often combined with a beta-blocker and diuretic when used to treat hypertension?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Hydralazine is a direct arteriolar vasodilator that decreases total peripheral resistance. This marked arterial dilation triggers two powerful compensatory reflex responses: 1) Baroreceptor-mediated sympathetic reflex activation, causing tachycardia and increased cardiac output (triggering palpitations or angina); 2) Decreased renal perfusion pressure and sympathetic activation of juxtaglomerular cells, activating RAAS and causing salt and water retention (pseudotolerance/edema). Co-administration of a beta-blocker (e.g. propranolol/metoprolol) blunts reflex tachycardia, and a diuretic (e.g. furosemide/thiazide) prevents fluid retention, creating synergistic blood pressure control.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q47: Why should beta-blockers be used with caution or avoided in patients with asthma or COPD?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Non-selective beta-blockers (such as propranolol) block beta-2 adrenergic receptors on bronchial smooth muscle cells, inhibiting physiological bronchodilation and precipitating severe, potentially life-threatening bronchospasm. Even cardioselective beta-1 blockers (e.g. metoprolol, atenolol) lose receptor selectivity at higher doses and should be used with extreme caution in asthmatic patients.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q48: Why might a healthcare provider prescribe an ACE inhibitor for a patient with heart failure?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
In heart failure, neurohormonal activation of the RAAS increases systemic vascular resistance (afterload) and fluid retention (preload), promoting adverse cardiac remodeling and ventricular dilation. ACE inhibitors block angiotensin II formation: 1) Reduce afterload by arterial vasodilation, improving cardiac output; 2) Reduce preload by venodilation and decreased aldosterone secretion; 3) Inhibit neurohormonally mediated cardiac myocyte hypertrophy, apoptosis, and myocardial fibrosis, conferring proven long-term survival and mortality reduction.

**Correct Answer:** -

---

### Q49: Why is it crucial to monitor serum potassium and renal function in patients taking spironolactone or eplerenone for severe heart failure?

**Type:** QROC

**Model Answer:**
Spironolactone and eplerenone are mineralocorticoid receptor antagonists (MRAs) that competitively block aldosterone receptors in the renal cortical collecting tubules. This inhibits Na+ reabsorption and K+/H+ excretion. When combined with ACE inhibitors or ARBs, there is a substantial risk of developing severe, life-threatening hyperkalemia (>5.5 mEq/L) and acute worsening of renal function. Routine monitoring of serum electrolytes and creatinine is essential to prevent fatal cardiac arrhythmias.

**Correct Answer:** -

### Q1: The followings drugs given alone are useful for treatment of hypertension EXCEPT:

- **A)** Amiodarone.
- **B)** Propranolol.
- **C)** Captopril
- **D)** Furosemide
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q2: Significant relaxation of arteriolar and venular smooth muscles can be pro-duced by which of the following drugs:

- **A)** Hydralazine
- **B)** Minoxidil
- **C)** Diazoxide
- **D)** Sodium nitroprusside
- **E)** Nifedipine

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q3: One of the following drugs is effective in treatment of ischemic episodes in patients with variant angina:

- **A)** Propranolol
- **B)** Nitroglycerine
- **C)** Sodium nitroprusside
- **D)** Diltiazem
- **E)** Hydralazines

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q4: The following is true about calcium channel blockers:

- **A)** Verapamil has a positive inotropic effect
- **B)** Verapamil dilates coronary arteries more than nifedipine
- **C)** Verapamil effectively relieves A-V block
- **D)** Diltiazem may cause ankle edema
- **E)** Nifedipine is contraindicated in Raynaud's disease

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q5: Negative inotropy and chronotropy are most prominent with the use of the following drug:

- **A)** Nifedipine
- **B)** Nicardipine
- **C)** Nimodipine
- **D)** Diltiazem
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q6: Which of the following mechanisms best explains the antihypertensive actions of clonidine

- **A)** Blockade of Î²1 adrenergic receptors
- **B)** Blockade of Î±1 adrenergic receptors
- **C)** Blockade of central Î±2 receptors
- **D)** Stimulation of presynaptic Î±2 receptors
- **E)** Stimulation of presynaptic Î²2 receptors

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q7: When glyceryl trinitrate (nitroglycerine) tablets are prescribed for angina pectoris, the patient should be told:

- **A)** To take it to prevent pain
- **B)** To take it at the onset of pain
- **C)** That if throbbing headache and palpita-tion occur the patient should take another
- **D)** That if he feels faint he should stand up perfectly
- **E)** To keep the tablet in a warm humid place e.g. a shelf over the bath

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q8: Which of the following antihypertensive drugs may cause fetal pulmonary hypoplasia and growth retardation if used during pregnancy:

- **A)** Hydralazine
- **B)** Alpha-methyldopa
- **C)** Atenolol
- **D)** Clonidine
- **E)** Captopril

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q9: All the following statements are true for nitroglycerine EXCEPT:

- **A)** Can cause adverse reactions of headache and tachycardia
- **B)** Undergoes significant first-pass biotransformation
- **C)** Can be used i.v. for the treatment of acute heart failure
- **D)** Has duration of action of several hours after sublingual administration
- **E)** Can increase exercise tolerance if taken immediately before exercise

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q10: A 57-year-old man is being treated for an atrial arrhythmia. He complains of headache, dizziness, and tinnitus. Which one of the following antiarrhythmic drugs is the most likely cause?

- **A)** Quinidine.
- **B)** Amiodarone.
- **C)** Procainamide.
- **D)** Propranolol.
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q11: A 58-year-old woman is being treated for chronic suppression of a ventricular arrhythmia. After 2 months of therapy, she complains about feeling tired all the time. Examination reveals a resting heart rate of 10 beats per minute lower than her previous rate. Her skin is cool and clammy. Laboratory test results indicate low thyroxin and elevated thyroid-stimulating hormone levels. Which of the following antiarrhythmic drugs is the likely cause of these signs and symptoms?

- **A)** Amiodarone.
- **B)** Procainamide.
- **C)** Propranolol.
- **D)** Quinidine.
- **E)** Verapamil.

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q12: A 56-year-old patient complains of chest pain following any sustained exercise. He is diagnosed with atherosclerotic angina. He is prescribed sublingual nitroglycerin for treatment of acute chest pain. Which of the following adverse effects is likely to be experienced by this patient?

- **A)** Hypertension.
- **B)** Throbbing headache.
- **C)** Bradycardia.
- **D)** Sexual dysfunction.
- **E)** Anemia.

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q13: A 68-year-old man has been successfully treated for exercise-induced angina for several years. He recently has been complaining about being awakened at night with chest pain. Which of the following drugs would be useful in preventing this patient's nocturnal angina?

- **A)** Amyl nitrite.
- **B)** Nitroglycerin (sublingual).
- **C)** Nitroglycerin (transdermal).
- **D)** Esmolol.
- **E)** Hydralazine.

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q14: Compensatory increases in heart rate and renin release that occur in heart failure may be alleviated by which of the following drugs?

- **A)** Milrinone.
- **B)** Digoxin.
- **C)** Dobutamine.
- **D)** Enalapril.
- **E)** Metoprolol.

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q15: A 58-year-old man is admitted to the hospital with acute heart failure and pulmonary edema. Which one of the following drugs would be most useful in treating the pulmonary edema?

- **A)** Digoxin.
- **B)** Dobutamine.
- **C)** Furosemide.
- **D)** Minoxidil.
- **E)** Spironolactone.

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q16: A 45-year-old man has recently been diagnosed with hypertension and started on monotherapy designed to reduce peripheral resistance and prevent NaCl and water retention. He has developed a persistent cough. Which of the following drugs would have the same benefits but would not cause cough?

- **A)** Losartan.
- **B)** Nifedipine.
- **C)** Prazosin.
- **D)** Propranolol.
- **E)** Verapamil.

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q17: Which one of the following drugs may cause a precipitous fall in blood pressure and fainting on initial administration?

- **A)** Atenolol.
- **B)** Hydrochlorothiazide.
- **C)** Nifedipine.
- **D)** Prazosin.
- **E)** Verapamil.

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q18: Which one of the following antihypertensive drugs can precipitate a hypertensive crisis following abrupt cessation of therapy?

- **A)** Clonidine.
- **B)** Diltiazem.
- **C)** Enalapril.
- **D)** Losartan.
- **E)** Hydrochlorothiazide.

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q19: Which of the following statements is most correct regarding the use of drugs in hypertension?

- **A)** beta-blockers are contraindicated if hypertension is associated with heart failure
- **B)** calcium channel blockers are contraindicated if the patient also has obstructive pulmonary disease
- **C)** diuretics are contraindicated in hypertension associated with diabetes
- **D)** vasodilators are contraindicated in a patient taking a diuretic
- **E)** ACE inhibitors are contraindicated if the patient is pregnant

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q20: B-Blockers have been effective in the treatment of heart failure. They primarily exert their effect by:

- **A)** Binding to the receptor that binds norepinephrine
- **B)** Inducing a prominent diuretic effect
- **C)** Increasing contractility
- **D)** Improving asthma control
- **E)** Increasing heart rate to meet the additional demands placed upon the heart in CHF

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q21: A patient has periodic episodes of paroxysmal supraventricular tachycardia (PSVT). Which of the following drugs would be most suitable for outpatient prophylaxis of these worrisome electrophysiologic events?

- **A)** Adenosine
- **B)** Lidocaine
- **C)** Nifedipine
- **D)** Nitroglycerin
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q22: We prescribe a beta-adrenergic blocker for a patient with chronic-stable (\"effort-induced\") angina, and the incidence and severity of anginal attacks are reduced. Which of the following best explains the pharmacologic action by which the beta blocker does this?

- **A)** Decreases myocardial oxygen demand
- **B)** Dilates the coronary vasculature
- **C)** Exerts antiplatelet/antithrombotic effects
- **D)** Reduces total peripheral resistance
- **E)** Slows AV nodal conduction velocity

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q23: Your patient is a 50-year-old man with essential hypertension and no other complications. Which of the following drugs would be the most rational first choice for starting his antihypertensive therapy?

- **A)** Angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE) inhibitor or angiotensin receptor blocker
- **B)** Beta-adrenergic blocker
- **C)** Nifedipine
- **D)** Thiazide diuretic
- **E)** Verapamil or diltiazem

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q24: We have a 50-year-old man with asymptomatic hyperuricemia, and we are about to start therapy for newly diagnosed essential hypertension (BP 136/90 mm Hg, based on repeated measurements with the patient supine and at rest). Which of the following antihypertensive drugs is most likely to increase his serum uric acid levels further, and possibly precipitate a gout attack?

- **A)** Captopril
- **B)** Hydrochlorothiazide
- **C)** Labetalol
- **D)** Losartan
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q25: We've just diagnosed essential hypertension in a 58-year-old female patient. She tends to be tachycardic. Notes written by her ophthalmologist indicate that she has chronic open-angle glaucoma. Which of the following drugs would be the most rational choice for this woman, given only the information presented in this question?

- **A)** Captopril
- **B)** Diltiazem
- **C)** Hydrochlorothiazide
- **D)** Timolol
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q26: Our newly diagnosed hypertensive patient has a history of vasospastic angina. Which of the following drugs or drug classes would be the most rational for starting antihypertensive therapy because it exerts antihypertensive effects, directly lowers myocardial oxygen demand and consumption, and also tends to inhibit cellular processes that otherwise favor coronary vasospasm?

- **A)** Angiotensin-converting enzyme (ACE) inhibitor or angiotensin receptor blocker
- **B)** Beta-Adrenergic blocker
- **C)** Nifedipine
- **D)** Thiazide diuretic
- **E)** Verapamil (or diltiazem)

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q27: Quinidine is ordered for a patient with recurrent atrial fibrillation and who refuses any interventions other than drugs in an attempt to terminate and control the arrhythmia. Which of the following is the most likely effect of quinidine?

- **A)** Is likely to increase blood pressure via a direct vasoconstrictor effect
- **B)** Is contraindicated if the patient also requires anticoagulant therapy
- **C)** Slows spontaneous SA nodal depolarization as its predominant effect
- **D)** Tends to slow electrical impulse conduction velocity through the AV node
- **E)** Will increase cardiac contractility (positive inotropic effect) independent of its antiarrhythmic effects

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q28: A patient who has been taking an oral antihypertensive drug for about a year develops a positive Coombs' test. Which of the following drugs is the most likely cause?

- **A)** Captopril
- **B)** Clonidine
- **C)** Labetalol
- **D)** Methyldopa
- **E)** Prazosin

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q29: A patient has Stage III essential hypertension. After evaluating the responses to several other antihypertensive drugs, alone and in combination, the physician places the patient on oral hydralazine. Which of the following adjunct(s) is/are likely to be needed to manage the expected and unwanted cardiovascular side effects of the hydralazine?

- **A)** Captopril plus nifedipine
- **B)** Digoxin plus spironolactone
- **C)** Digoxin plus vitamin K
- **D)** Hydrochlorothiazide and a beta blocker
- **E)** Triamterene plus amiloride

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q30: At high (but not necessarily toxic) blood levels, a cardiovascular drug causes lightheadedness, tinnitus, and visual disturbances such as diplopia. What is the most likely drug that caused these responses?

- **A)** Atropine
- **B)** Captopril
- **C)** Dobutamine
- **D)** Propranolol
- **E)** Quinidine

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q31: We have a patient who is diagnosed with variant (vasospastic) angina. Which of the following drugs would be most appropriate, and generally regarded as most effective, for long-term therapy aimed at reducing the incidence or severity of the coronary vasospasm?

- **A)** Aspirin
- **B)** Atorvastatin
- **C)** Diltiazem
- **D)** Nitroglycerin
- **E)** Propranolol

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q32: The following treatments can be applied for rapid relief of acute anginal pain EXCEPT:

- **A)** Sublingual nitroglycerine
- **B)** Sublingual isosorbide dinitrate
- **C)** Oral aspirin
- **D)** Inhaled amylnitrite
- **E)** Intravenous nitroglycerine

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q33: Which of the following statements is true concerning the action of enalapril:

- **A)** It is prodrug for an angiotensin II blocker
- **B)** Stimulates angiotensin II receptors
- **C)** Increases endogenous bradykinin levels
- **D)** Does not lower blood pressure in \"normal renin hypertension
- **E)** Best indicated to patients with hyper-tension associated with liver disease

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q34: Beta-blockers are considered the first choice in the control hypertension in the following cases EXCEPT:

- **A)** Hypertension with thyrotoxicosis
- **B)** Hypertension with ventricular premature beats
- **C)** Hypertension with variant angina
- **D)** Hypertension with high plasma renin
- **E)** Young patient with increased sympathetic activity

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q35: Severe myocardial pain due to acute inferior Ml with excessive vagal stimulation can be best managed by:

- **A)** Morphine
- **B)** Meperidine
- **C)** Butorphanol
- **D)** Diclofenac
- **E)** Indomethacin

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q36: The enhancement of myocardial contraction after digitalis is due to:

- **A)** Increased cyclic AMP
- **B)** Stimulation of calmodulin
- **C)** Beta-adrenergic stimulation
- **D)** Increased production of adenosine
- **E)** Inhibition of the sodium pump

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q37: An ideal drug for treatment of ventricular arrhythmia due to acute myocardial Infarction is:

- **A)** Propranolol
- **B)** Lidocaine
- **C)** Quinidine
- **D)** Procainamide
- **E)** Verapamil

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q38: Digoxin is not indicated to treat heart failure as long systemic hypertension is present because:

- **A)** The heart may develop more strain and failure
- **B)** Absorption of digoxin will be reduced
- **C)** Excretion of digoxin will be reduced
- **D)** Liability for digoxin toxicity is high
- **E)** Systemic hypertension will be reduced suddenly

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q39: Wolff-Parkinson-White syndrome can be successfully treated by:

- **A)** Quinidine
- **B)** Verapamil
- **C)** Atenolol
- **D)** Digoxin
- **E)** Amiodarone

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q40: All the following are positive inotropic drugs EXCEPT:

- **A)** Digitalis
- **B)** Adrenaline
- **C)** Aminophylline
- **D)** Dopamine
- **E)** Amiodarone

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q41: All the following are class I antiarrhythmic drugs EXCEPT:

- **A)** Lidocaine
- **B)** Quinidine
- **C)** Propranolol
- **D)** Phenytoin
- **E)** Procainamide

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q42: Which of the following cardiac glycosides has the clearly higher therapeutic index:

- **A)** Quabain.
- **B)** Digoxin.
- **C)** Digitoxin.
- **D)** Deslanoside
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q43: The ECG of a patient who is receiving digitalis in the therapeutic range would be most likely to show:

- **A)** Prolongation of the OT interval.
- **B)** Prolongation of the PR interval
- **C)** Symmetrical peaking of the T-wave
- **D)** Widening of the QRS complex
- **E)** Deep Q wave

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q44: If both quinidine and digoxin are administered concurrently, which of the following effects does quinidine have on digoxin?

- **A)** The absorption of digoxin from the GIT is decreased
- **B)** The metabolism of digoxin is prevented
- **C)** The concentration of digoxin in the plasma is increased
- **D)** The excretion of digoxin is increased
- **E)** The ability of digoxin to inhibit N+/K+ ATPase is reduced

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q45: All of the following are considered mechanisms of antiarrhythmic drugs EXCEPT:

- **A)** Calcium channel blockade
- **B)** Inhibition of catecholamine action on the heart
- **C)** Sodium channel blockade
- **D)** Increasing the slope of phase 4 depolarization
- **E)** Prolongation of effective refractory period (action potential duration)

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q46: All the following are side effects of amiodarone EXCEPT:

- **A)** Corneal microdeposits
- **B)** Tachycardia
- **C)** Thyroid dysfunction
- **D)** Pulmonary infiltration
- **E)** Hepatotoxicity

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q47: Digitalis toxicity is enhanced by which one of the following:

- **A)** Decreased extracellular Ca++
- **B)** Decreased stimulation rate
- **C)** Increased extracellular Mg++
- **D)** Decreased extracellular K+
- **E)** Decreased extracellular Na+

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q48: The most specific line for treatment of digitalis toxicity is:

- **A)** Stop digitalis administration
- **B)** Administration of potassium
- **C)** Administration of Fab fragments
- **D)** Administration of phenytoin
- **E)** Administration of calcium

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q49: The usefulness of digitalis in management of atrial fibrillation depends on its ability to

- **A)** Decrease atrial impulse formation
- **B)** Decrease vagal control over the heart
- **C)** Decrease conduction time through the A-V node
- **D)** Increase the effective refractory pe the A-V node
- **E)** Increase conduction time in the atria

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q50: Quinidine is either contraindicated or should be used with caution in all of the following EXCEPT:

- **A)** Complete atrioventricular block.
- **B)** Digitalis intoxication
- **C)** Severe congestive heart failure
- **D)** Atrial fibrillation of recent origin
- **E)** History of idiosyncrasy due to quindine

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q51: A 45-year-old man asks his physician for a prescription for sildenafil to improve his sex performance. Because of risks from a serious drug interaction, this drug should not be prescribed, and the patient should be urged not to try to obtain it from other sources, if h also taking which of the following drugs?

- **A)** An angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitor
- **B)** A beta-adrenergic blocker
- **C)** A nitrovasodilator (e.g., nitroglycerin)
- **D)** A statin-type antihypercholesterolemic drug
- **E)** A thiazide or loop diuretic

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q52: A 70-year-old woman is treated with sublingual nitroglycerin for occasional bouts of effort-induced angina. Which of the following best describes the mechanism by which nitroglycerin causes its desired antianginal effects, or a mediator involved in it?

- **A)** Blocks beta adrenergic receptors
- **B)** Forms cyanide, much like the metabolism of nitroprusside does
- **C)** Increases local synthesis and release of adenosine
- **D)** Raises intracellular cGMP levels
- **E)** Stimulates phosphodiesterase

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q53: All of the following are recommended at the initial stages of treating patients with heart failure EXCEPT:

- **A)** Reduced salt intake
- **B)** Verapamil
- **C)** ACE inhibitors
- **D)** Diuretics
- **E)** Vasodilators

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q54: Which one of the following drugs increases digoxin plasma concentration by a pharmacokinetic mechanism?

- **A)** Captopril
- **B)** Hydrochorothiazide
- **C)** Lidocaine
- **D)** Quinidine
- **E)** Sulfasalazine

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q55: Regarding verapamil, which one of the following statements is false?

- **A)** Angina pectoris is an important indication for the use of verapamil
- **B)** Contraindicated in the asthmatic patient
- **C)** Relaxes vascular smooth muscle
- **D)** Slows the depolarization phase of the action potential in AV nodal cells
- **E)** Used in management of supraventricular tachycardias

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q56: Exertion-induced angina, which is relieved by rest, nitroglycerin, or both, is referred to as:

- **A)** Prinzmetal's angina
- **B)** Unstable angina
- **C)** Classic angina
- **D)** Variant angina
- **E)** Preinfarction angina

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q57: Which of the following drugs is a class IV antiarrhythmic that is primarily indicated for the treatment of supraventricular tachyarrhythmias?

- **A)** Diltiazem
- **B)** Digoxin
- **C)** Mexiletine
- **D)** Quinidine
- **E)** Propranolol

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q1: In the above diagram, the following phase is the phase at which there a rapid influx of Na+

- **A)** Phase 0
- **B)** Phase 1
- **C)** Phase 2
- **D)** Phase 3
- **E)** Phase 4

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q2: In the above diagram, the following phase is very important for determining the mechanical contractility power of the cardiac muscle

- **A)** Phase 0
- **B)** Phase 1
- **C)** Phase 2
- **D)** Phase 3
- **E)** Phase 4

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q3: From the above cardiac mechanical curve, it is impossible to tetanize a heart because

- **A)** The heart muscles do not contain Ca2+
- **B)** There is a long refractory period
- **C)** The mechanical contraction is shorter than the duration of electrical depolarization
- **D)** There's a short refractory period

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q4: Which of the following conditions at the A-V node will cause a decrease in heart rate?

- **A)** Increased sodium permeability
- **B)** Decreased acetylcholine levels
- **C)** Increased norepinephrine levels
- **D)** Increased potassium permeability
- **E)** Increased calcium permeability

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q5: Which of the following is true regarding the (phase 2) of the ventricular action potential?

- **A)** Ca2+ from the sarcoplasmic reticulum diffuses out of the cell, causing repolarization
- **B)** Extracellular Ca2+ diffuses into the cardiac muscle cells, binds to troponin and initiates contraction
- **C)** Na+ channels remain open causing the continued depolarization
- **D)** Opening of fast Na+ channels in the membranes of contractile cells results in a depolarization

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q6: Which of these statements concerning contractile cardiac cells is correct?

- **A)** Contractile cardiac cells resting membrane potential is around -55mV
- **B)** Contractile cardiac cells have a prolonged period of delayed repolarization called the plateau phase
- **C)** Contractile cardiac cells has a short refractory period
- **D)** Depolarization of contractile cardiac cells occurs when K+ diffuse into the cell

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q7: Which of these conditions occur in the cardiac muscle cell during the plateau phase?

- **A)** voltage-gated Ca2+ ion (L channels) are open
- **B)** voltage-gated Na+ ion channels are open
- **C)** voltage-gated K+ ion channels are closed
- **D)** voltage-gated Ca2+ ion channels are closed

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q8: A 45 old lady was subjected to surgery, then about 3 liters of intra-venous fluid was given within one hour, this led to increase in stroke volume and increase in cardiac output. With respect to the effect on stroke volume, the cause of increased stroke volume in this patient will be due to:

- **A)** Increased afterload
- **B)** Increased stretch of myocardial cells
- **C)** Increased autonomic nervous stimulation
- **D)** Increased cardiac contractility

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q9: In the above diagram area B represent the unstable resting membrane potential of the pacemaker potential which indicates the activity of ...

- **A)** sodium, and calcium L- channels
- **B)** sodium, and calcium T- channels
- **C)** potassium and calcium T- channels
- **D)** potassium and calcium L- channels

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q10: Which of the following would cause the change from curve B to curve A in pacemaker potential in the above diagram ...?

- **A)** Acetylcholine
- **B)** Norepinephrine
- **C)** Intracellular acidosis
- **D)** Ca2+ channel blockers

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q11: Propagation of the action potential through the heart is fastest in

- **A)** SA node
- **B)** atrial muscle
- **C)** AV node
- **D)** Purkinje fibers
- **E)** ventricular muscle

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q12: The spread of cardiac excitation is delayed by about 0.1 s at...

- **A)** Atrio-ventricular node
- **B)** Bundle of His
- **C)** Sino-atrial node
- **D)** Purkinje fibers

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q13: A 50-year-old woman has a resting heart rate of 70 beats/min arterial pressure is 130/85 mm Hg, and body temperature is normal. From the above diagram, Her Stroke Volume in milliliters is ...

- **A)** 50
- **B)** 100
- **C)** 150
- **D)** 200

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q14: The period of ventricular ejection lies between the ....

- **A)** Between point A and point B
- **B)** Between point B and point C
- **C)** Between point C and point D
- **D)** Between point D and point A

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q15: The 3rd heart sound occurs in the period that lies between.....

- **A)** Between point A and point B
- **B)** Between point B and point C
- **C)** Between point C and point D
- **D)** Between point D and point A

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q16: Which of the following phases in the above curve are iso-volumetric phases?

- **A)** Phase Number 1&2
- **B)** Phase Number 2&5
- **C)** Phase Number 3&4
- **D)** Phase Number 1&6
- **E)** Phase Number 3&6

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q17: Which of the following phase in the above curve shows the sharp rise in the Aortic pressure?

- **A)** Phase Number 1
- **B)** Phase Number 2
- **C)** Phase Number 3
- **D)** Phase Number 4
- **E)** Phase Number 5

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q18: in the above curve; the 4 shows fluctuation between.

- **A)** 40-80 mmHg
- **B)** 60-100 mmHg
- **C)** 80-120 mmHg
- **D)** 100-140 mmHg

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q19: Which of the following events occurs at the beginning of the period of rapid ventricular ejection?

- **A)** Aortic and Pulmonary valves close
- **B)** Mitral valve opens
- **C)** Tricuspid valve opens
- **D)** Aortic and Pulmonary valves open

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q20: A phase shows the onset of a marked rise in pressure of the left ventricle; what is the name of this phase?

- **A)** Isometric contraction phase
- **B)** Rapid ejection phase
- **C)** Slow ejection phase
- **D)** Rapid ventricular filling
- **E)** Reduced ventricular filling

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q21: What is the cause of this marked rise in pressure in this phase?

- **A)** Closure of all valves while the heart relaxes
- **B)** Closure of all valves while the heart contracts
- **C)** Opening of all valves while heart contracts
- **D)** Opening of all valves while heart relaxes

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q22: Which of the following events occurs at the beginning of the period of rapid ventricular filling?

- **A)** V valves close
- **B)** Aortic valve opens
- **C)** Pulmonary valve opens
- **D)** V valves open

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q23: From the curve, the ventricles are maximally filled by the end of which of the following phase:

- **A)** Atrial systole phase
- **B)** Rapid ejection phase
- **C)** Slow ejection phase
- **D)** Rapid ventricular filling
- **E)** Reduced ventricular filling

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q24: A medical student recorded the changes in ventricular pressure in relation to change in volume, and he draw (the above curve) the response in stroke volume in relation to increased contractility. The increased contractility will lead to a decrease in:

- **A)** Cardiac output
- **B)** End diastolic volume
- **C)** End systolic volume
- **D)** Stroke volume
