---

### Q110: Which of the following are caused by acetylcholine?

- **A)** Hyperpolarization of the S-A node
- **B)** Depolarization of the A-V node
- **C)** Decreased permeability of the S-A node to potassium ions
- **D)** Increased heart rate
- **E)** Increased permeability of the cardiac muscle to calcium ions

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q111: Which of the following best explains how sympathetic stimulation affects the heart?

- **A)** Permeability of the S-A node to sodium decreases
- **B)** Permeability of the A-V node to sodium decreases
- **C)** Permeability of the S-A node to potassium increases
- **D)** There is an increased rate of upward drift of the resting membrane potential of the S-A node
- **E)** Permeability of the cardiac muscle to calcium decreases

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q112: A 49 years old lady brought to the ER with severe diarrhea and vomiting. O am examination she was confused with cold clammy skin, pulse rate 110/min., blood pressure 80/50 mmHg, respiratory rate 25/min., and temperature 37.1 C. The most likely diagnosis is:

- **A)** Anaphylactic shock
- **B)** Cardiogenic shock
- **C)** Hypovolemic shock
- **D)** Septic shock

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q113: You are asked to see a 69 year old unconscious male patient who was brought to the ER by ambulance. He looks pale and his peripheries are cold and clammy. On examination: pulse rate 60/bpm; blood pressure 70/45 mmHg; his central venous pressure was raised. The most likely diagnosis is:

- **A)** Anaphylactic shock
- **B)** Cardiogenic shock
- **C)** Hypovolemic shock
- **D)** Septic shock

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q114: A 23 years old male presented to the ER with inability to breath, rapid, weak pulse, very low blood pressure with shallow rapid breathing. He had warm red limbs and severe itching. His relatives mentioned that he had penicillin injection. This patient is probably having

- **A)** Cardiogenic shock
- **B)** Distributive shock
- **C)** Hypovolemic shock
- **D)** Obstructive shock

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q115: The underlying pathophysiology of cardiovascular signs in this patient is most probably

- **A)** Generalized vasodilation and reflex tachycardia
- **B)** Generalized vasoconstriction and reflex tachycardia
- **C)** Autonomic failure leading to hypovolemia
- **D)** Autonomic failure leading to hypoxemia

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q116: In this patient; there is a release of large amount of ...

- **A)** Histamine
- **B)** Bradykinin
- **C)** Nitric oxide
- **D)** Interleukin-1

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q117: A 20-year-old man is brought to a local emergency room with severe haemorrhage after motor-car accident. He has cold pale sweaty skin, increased heart rate, and arterial pressure of 80/40. One of the rapid compensatory mechanisms that rapidly operated to restore this patient condition is:

- **A)** CNS ischemic response
- **B)** Decreased glomerular filtration rate
- **C)** Aldosterone release
- **D)** Atrial natriuritic peptide (ANP) release

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q118: A healthy 36-year-old man experiences a sudden dizziness and loss consciousness when he stands from a supine position. The cause of this condition is a sudden fall in:

- **A)** Coronary blood flow
- **B)** Cerebral blood pressure and cerebral blood flow
- **C)** Splanchnic circulation
- **D)** Lower limb blood flow

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q119: A doctor suspects preeclampsia in a 28-year-old pregnant, hypertensive woman complaining from weight gain and swelling in her face, hands, legs of sudden onset. She also notes polyuria and proteinuria. In this patient, the underlying pathophysiology of edema is most probably

- **A)** Increased capillary hydrostatic pressure.
- **B)** Decreased plasma colloid osmotic pressure.
- **C)** Decreased filtration coefficient.
- **D)** Blockage of lymphatics.

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q120: The type of edema in this patient is

- **A)** Extracellular, pitting edema
- **B)** Intracellular, pitting edema
- **C)** Extracellular, non-pitting edema
- **D)** Intracellular, non-pitting edema

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q121: A 45 years man suffering from severe edema of his arm and hand after surgical removal of a malignant axillary lymph nodes. The underlying pathophysiology of edema in this patient, is most probably

- **A)** Increased capillary hydrostatic pressure.
- **B)** Decreased plasma colloid osmotic pressure.
- **C)** Decreased filtration coefficient.
- **D)** Inadequate lymph drainage.

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q122: A 35-years old female admitted to the intensive care unit after She was severely bleeding, unconscious; her skin was pale and and cold. Her vital signs were: Pulse 43, RR 7, and BP 70/40. After 3 days of receiving fluids and medication there was no improvement in blood pressure. This patient is probably in what stage of shock?

- **A)** Irreversible shock
- **B)** Compensated shock
- **C)** Reversible shock
- **D)** Decompensated shock

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q123: The cause of unresponsiveness to medication is most probably due to ...

- **A)** Depressed cardiac inhibitory center
- **B)** Depressed expiratory center
- **C)** Depressed vasoconstrictor center
- **D)** Depressed inspiratory center

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q124: A 56 year old male presented with severe chest pain of 2 hours duration and was admitted as a case of acute myocardial infarction in intensive cardiac care of the hospital.On the 3rd day, the doctor noticed raised JVP, hepatomegaly and lower limb edema .BP was 90/60 mm Hg. What is the most likely cause of his signs?

- **A)** Cardiogenic shock
- **B)** Anaphylactic shock
- **C)** Neurogenic shock
- **D)** Septic shock

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q125: Regarding the conductive system of the heart

- **A)** both SAN and AVN are innervated by the left vagus nerve
- **B)** there is a delay of about 0.2 second at A-V node.
- **C)** Purkinje fibres have the shortest duration of action potential.
- **D)** the ventricular muscle has the fastest rate of conduction

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q126: If the hydrostatic pressure in a capillary is 10 mm Hg, interstitial pressure is 2 mm Hg, colloidal osmotic pressure of plasma is 25 mm Hg and interstitial fluid colloid osmotic pressure is 5 mm Hg. Which of the following is correct for this situation?

- **A)** filtration with a net pressure of 18 mm Hg.
- **B)** filtration with a net pressure of 12 mm Hg.
- **C)** reasbsorption with a net pressure of 18 mm Hg
- **D)** reabsorption with a net pressure of 12 mm Hg.

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q127: Which of the following cannot be associated with Oedema?

- **B)** an increase in venous pressure.
- **C)** a decrease in plasma oncotic pressure
- **D)** blokcage of lymph ducts

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q128: The mechanism of decrease in the end-systolic volume of the left ventricle and increase in stroke volume during exercise is the following:

- **A)** Starling law of the heart
- **B)** an increase in sympathetic tone to the ventricle
- **C)** an increase in parasympathetic tone to the ventricle
- **D)** a decrease in venous return of blood to the heart

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q129: Venous return to the heart is increased by which of the following?

- **A)** by rise of mean systemic filling pressure
- **B)** in anaphylactic shock
- **C)** during deep expiration against closed glottis
- **D)** in pneumothorax

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q130: Increased baroreceptor discharge causes which of the following?

- **A)** increases the tonic discharge of the vasoconstrictor nerves
- **B)** inhibits the vagal innervations of the heart
- **C)** produces vasodilation, venodilation and a drop in BP
- **D)** induces tachycardia and an increase in COP

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q131: Stretch of the atria causes which of the following?

- **A)** reflex constriction of the afferent arteriole of the kidney
- **B)** increase the secretion of vasopressin (ADH)
- **C)** increase secretion of ANP
- **D)** increase secretion of renin

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q132: Which of the following about dicrotic notch of the aortic blood pressure curve is correct?

- **A)** is produced by contraction of the left ventricle
- **B)** is produced by sudden closure of the aortic valve
- **C)** marks the beginning of the isovolumetric contraction phase
- **D)** associates with the third heart sound

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q133: Turbulent flow in an artery is induced by which of the following?

- **A)** a decrease in velocity of blood
- **B)** a decrease in the viscosity of the blood
- **C)** an increase in hematocrit value
- **D)** a decrease in diameter of blood vessel

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q134: Digitalis has positive inotropic effect through which of the following mechanism?

- **A)** an increase of intracellular cAMP
- **B)** activation of voltage-gated calcium channels
- **C)** inhibition of Na+-K+ ATPase in the myocardium
- **D)** inhibition of ATP-dependent Ca2+ pump in the sarcoplasmic reticulum

### Q135: Sympathetic stimulation to the heart results in which of the following?

- **A)** an increase in the duration of systole
- **B)** an increase in the duration of diastole

- **D)** a decrease in the affinity of troponin for calcium

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q136: A patient suffers blood loss over a period of 30 minutes. At the end, arterial blood pressure was 70/50, his heart rate was 145/min, hematocrit was 35%. Which of the following conditions would not be expected to occur in this situation?

- **B)** an increased plasma colloid osmotic pressure
- **C)** a decreased capillary hydrostatic pressure
- **D)** an increased catecholamine secretion

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q137: The arterial baroreceptor reflex involves which of the following?

- **B)** increased tonic discharge of vasoconstrictor centre to arterioles and venules
- **C)** excitation of vagal innervations to the heart

### Q138: Which of the following functions is incorrect regarding stimulation of angiotensin II receptors AT1?

### Q139: In Cushing reflex which of the followings is incorrect?

- **C)** acceleration of the heart

### Q140: Which of the following about Cerebral blood flow is correct?

- **D)** decreases when there is a high arterial PCO2

### Q141: Which of the following Cardiovascular responses to isotonic (dynamic exercise) is incorrect?

### Q142: Peirpheral resistance is:

### Q143: A decrease in the carotid sinus pressure from 100 mmHg will lead to which of the following?

- **B)** a reflex stimulation of cardiac sympathetic nerves

- **D)** an increase of venous capacitance

### Q144: Regarding Low resistance shock which of the following is correct?

- **B)** is characterized by an increase of the venous return

### Q145: The following data were collected from an individual: Pulse rate = 100 beats/min, B.P. = 120/80, Oxygen consumption = 1500 ml/5 min, Pulmonary artery O2 content = 14 ml/100 ml, Aortic artery O2 content = 20 ml/100 ml. The stroke volume of this individual is:

### Q146: About Cardiac reserve which of the following is correct?

### Q147: Normal splitting of the second heart sound is increased during inspiration because:

### Q148: The negative chronotropic effect of vagal stimulation is due to:

### Q150: Right artial pressure (central venous pressure):

### Q152: Which of the following statements about the control of the peripheral circulation and blood pressure is true?

### Q155: Concerning regulation of the coronary blood flow which of the following is true

- **B)** stimulation of the vagal fibers to the heart dilates coronary vessels

### Q156: Which part of the ECG corresponds to ventricular repolarization?

### Q157: Which of the following normally has a slowly depolarizing \"prepotential\"

### Q159: The second heart sound is caused by which of the following?

### Q161: During exercise, a man consumes 1.8 L of oxygen per minute. His arterial O2 content is 190 mL/L, and the O2 content of his mixed venous blood is 134 mL/L. His cardiac output is approximately

- **E)** the afterload is greater

### Q163: Which of the following about Starling's law of the heart is true?

### Q166: The pressure in a capillary in skeletal muscle is 35 mm Hg at the arteriolar end and 14 mm Hg at the venular end. The interstitial pressure is 0 mm Hg. - **A)** 3 mm Hg out of the capillary

- **E)** 11 mm Hg into the capillary

### Q169: A 30-year-old patient comes to her primary care clinician complaining of headaches and vertigo. A blood test reveals a hematocrit of 55%, and a diagnosis of polycythemia is made. Which of the following would also be increased?

### Q172: A 29-year-old top endurance athlete completes a medical examination as part of her registration for a triathlon event. Her resting HR is measured at 36 beats/min. - **D)** Increased automatic rate of SA node discharge

### Q174: An 80-year-old man visits a new family physician. He has not visited a physician for 15 years, but is presently having trouble sleeping and is seeking a prescription for sleeping pills. - **B)** B

- **D)** D

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q195: Phase-4 depolarization of SA nodal cells is caused by

- **A)** An increase in the flow of sodium into the cell
- **B)** A decrease in the flow of potassium out of the cell
- **C)** An increase in the activity of the Na/Ca exchanger
- **D)** A decrease in the flow of chloride out of the cell
- **E)** A decrease in the activity of the Na-K pump

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q196: Cardiovascular changes that occur during inspiration include decreased which of the following?

- **A)** Right ventricular filling
- **B)** Right ventricular output
- **C)** Pressure gradient from extrathoracic veins to the right atrium
- **D)** Systemic blood pressure
- **E)** Left ventricular contractility

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q197: Blood pressure increases and heart rate decreases in response to which of the following?

- **A)** Exercise
- **B)** Increased body temperature
- **C)** Exposure to high altitude
- **D)** Increased intracranial pressure
- **E)** Hemorrhage

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q198: During exercise, cardiac output is augmented by which of the following?

- **A)** Sympathetic stimulation of resistance vessels
- **B)** Dilation of venous vessels
- **C)** Decreased end-diastolic volume
- **D)** Decreased mean systemic arterial pressure
- **E)** Increased ventricular contractility

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q199: Stroke volume can be decreased by which of the following?

- **A)** Increasing ventricular contractility
- **B)** Increasing heart rate
- **C)** Increasing central venous pressure
- **D)** Decreasing total peripheral resistance
- **E)** Decreasing systemic blood pressure

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q200: Stroke volume can be increased by which of the following?

- **A)** Decreasing ventricular compliance
- **B)** Increasing venous compliance
- **C)** Decreasing total peripheral resistance
- **D)** Increasing heart rate
- **E)** Decreasing atrial contractility

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q201: Which one of the following correctly describes an event that normally occurs during the PR interval?

- **A)** The ventricle is contracting
- **B)** The cardiac action potential passes through the AV node
- **C)** There is no change in the voltage tracing on the ECG
- **D)** The mitral and aortic valves are both closed
- **E)** The second heart sound is heard

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q202: Which one of the following will be observed in a patient with aortic regurgitation?

- **A)** An increased ejection fraction
- **B)** An increased mean blood pressure
- **C)** An increased diastolic blood pressure
- **D)** A decreased pulse pressure
- **E)** A decreased left atrial end-diastolic pressure

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q203: The upstroke of the SA nodal action potential is produced by opening a channel that is

- **A)** Primarily permeable to Na+
- **B)** Primarily permeable to Ca2+
- **C)** Primarily permeable to K+
- **D)** Primarily permeable to Cl-
- **E)** Equally permeable to Na+ and K+

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q204: The channel responsible for the initiation of phase-4 depolarization in SA nodal cells

- **A)** Is primarily permeable to Na+
- **B)** Is opened by membrane depolarization
- **C)** Is opened by vagal nerve stimulation
- **D)** Is primarily permeable to K+
- **E)** Is closed by norepinephrine

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q205: Sympathetic stimulation of the heart results in which of the following?

- **A)** An increase in the activity of the SR calcium pump
- **B)** An increase in the duration of systole
- **C)** An increase in the duration of diastole
- **D)** A decrease in the affinity of troponin for calcium
- **E)** A decrease in the concentration of Ca2 during systole

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q206: After a mild hemorrhage, compensatory responses initiated by the baroreceptor reflex keep blood pressure at or close to its normal value. Which one of the following values is less after compensation for the hemorrhage than it was before the hemorrhage?

- **A)** Venous compliance
- **B)** Heart rate
- **C)** Ventricular contractility
- **D)** Total peripheral resistance
- **E)** Coronary blood flow

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q207: The constriction of a blood vessel to one-half of its resting diameter would increase its resistance to blood flow by a factor of

- **A)** 2
- **B)** 4
- **C)** 12
- **D)** 16

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q208: During aerobic exercise, blood flow remains relatively constant within

- **A)** The skin
- **B)** The heart
- **C)** The brain
- **D)** The skeletal muscles
- **E)** The kidneys

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q209: Sudden standing evokes the baroreceptor reflex. Which one of the following will be greater after a person suddenly stands up than it was before the person stood?

- **A)** The end-diastolic volume
- **B)** The renal blood flow
- **C)** The venous return
- **D)** The pulse pressure
- **E)** The ejection fraction

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q210: Central venous pressure is increased by which of the following?

- **A)** Decreasing blood volume
- **B)** Increasing venous compliance
- **C)** Increasing total peripheral resistance
- **D)** Decreasing heart rate
- **E)** Decreasing plasma aldosterone concentration

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q211: Capillary permeability is lowest within which of the following?

- **A)** Kidneys
- **B)** Spleen
- **C)** Liver
- **D)** Brain
- **E)** Skin

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q212: Blood flow through an organ would be increased by decreasing which of the following?

- **A)** The diameter of the arterial vessels
- **B)** The number of open arterial vessels
- **C)** The arterial pressure
- **D)** The diameter of the venous vessels
- **E)** The hematocrit

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q213: Which of the following will increased net filtration from capillaries?

- **A)** Increased contraction of the precapillary sphincter
- **B)** Decreased arterial pressure
- **C)** Increased plasma protein concentration
- **D)** Decreased capillary permeability
- **E)** Increased postcapillary resistance

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q214: A reduction in carotid sinus pressure would cause a decrease in which of the following?

- **A)** Heart rate
- **B)** Myocardial contractility
- **C)** Total peripheral resistance
- **D)** Venous compliance
- **E)** Cardiac output

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q215: At which of the following sites does the blood flow lose the greatest amount of energy?

- **A)** Mitral valve
- **B)** Large arteries
- **C)** Arterioles
- **D)** Capillaries
- **E)** Venules

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q216: Which one of the following decreases during aerobic exercise?

- **A)** Circulating blood volume
- **B)** Heart rate
- **C)** Skin temperature
- **D)** Cerebral blood flow
- **E)** Mean blood pressure

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q217: Based on the following values, the flow of fluid out of the capillaries will be zero if the average interstitial hydrostatic pressure is: Average capillary hydrostatic pressure = 18 mmHg, Average capillary oncotic pressure = 27 mmHg, Average interstitial oncotic pressure = 7 mmHg

- **A)** -4 mmHg
- **B)** -2 mmHg
- **C)** 0 mmHg
- **D)** +1 mmHg
- **E)** +2 mmHg

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q218: Which one of the following characteristics is most similar in the systemic and pulmonary circulations?

- **A)** Stroke work
- **B)** Preload
- **C)** Afterload
- **D)** Peak systolic pressure
- **E)** Blood flow

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q219: The ratio of the patient's total peripheral resistance before administration of the drug to the total peripheral resistance after administration of the drug is

- **A)** 1:1
- **B)** 3:2
- **C)** 4:3
- **D)** 5:4
- **E)** 6:5

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q220: Which one of the following values is greater in the pulmonary circulation than in systemic circulation?

- **A)** The mean arterial pressure
- **B)** The arterial resistance
- **C)** The vascular compliance
- **D)** The blood flow
- **E)** The sympathetic tone

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q221: The greatest percentage of blood volume is found in which of the following?

- **A)** Heart
- **B)** Aorta
- **C)** Distributing arteries and arterioles
- **D)** Capillaries
- **E)** Venules and veins

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q222: Turbulence is more likely to occur in a blood vessel if

- **A)** The velocity of blood within the vessel increases
- **B)** The viscosity of blood within the vessel increases
- **C)** The diameter of the vessel decreases
- **D)** The density of the blood decreases
- **E)** The length of the vessel increases

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q223: Systemic arteriolar constriction may result from an increase in the local concentration of which of the following?

- **A)** Nitric oxide
- **B)** Angiotensin II
- **C)** Atrial natriuretic peptide
- **D)** Beta agonists
- **E)** Hydrogen ion

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q224: Which of the following will be lower than normal in a patient with an abnormally high intracranial pressure?

- **A)** Ventricular contractility
- **B)** Heart rate
- **C)** Mean blood pressure
- **D)** Stroke volume
- **E)** Total peripheral resistance

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q225: Flow of fluid through the lymphatic vessels will be decreased if there is an increase in which of the following?

- **A)** Capillary pressure
- **B)** Capillary permeability
- **C)** Interstitial protein concentration
- **D)** Capillary oncotic pressure

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q226: The above figure shows a schematic representation of a left ventricular volume loop. During which interval are all the heart valves closed?

- **A)** B
- **B)** C
- **C)** D
- **D)** E

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q227: An increase in arterial baroreceptor firing rate will reflexly result in which of the following?

- **A)** an increase in vagal activity to the heart
- **B)** an increase in sympathetic activity to arterioles in the brain
- **C)** an increase in renal blood flow
- **D)** an increase in mean arterial pressure
- **E)** an increase in cardiac ejection fraction

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q228: The below figure shows a single cardiac action potential measured in a normal ventricular myocyte. Which of the following stable ionic currents across the cell membrane is most responsible for phase 4?

- **A)** Ca2+ current
- **B)** Cl- current
- **C)** K+ current
- **D)** Na+ current

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q229: Which of the following will produce the greatest increase in blood flow through exercising muscles?

- **A)** halve the length of the capillaries
- **B)** halve the viscosity of the blood
- **C)** double the radius of the venules
- **D)** double the blood pressure difference across the bed
- **E)** double the radius of the arterioles

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q230: Calculate the cardiac output from the following data: pulmonary arterial pressure = 20 mm Hg; left atrial pressure = 5 mm Hg; pulmonary vascular resistance = 3 mm Hg per L/min.

- **A)** 3 L/min
- **B)** 5 L/min
- **C)** 4 L/min
- **D)** 15 L/min
- **E)** 60 mm Hg

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q231: The primary route of removal of [Ca2+] from the sarcoplasm during relaxation of cardiac muscle cell is by

- **A)** active transport out of the cell
- **B)** passive exchange with extracellular sodium
- **C)** active transport into the sarcoplasmic reticulum
- **D)** trapping of calcium by troponin in the myofilaments
- **E)** passive movement out of the cell via L-type calcium channels

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q232: A therapeutic strategy to improve the amount of active shortening of cardiac muscle might include which one of the following?

- **A)** decrease preload
- **B)** decrease afterload
- **C)** decrease contractility
- **D)** give a negative chronotropic agent
- **E)** give a negative inotropic agent

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q233: Arteriolar constriction tends to do which of the following?

- **A)** decrease total peripheral resistance
- **B)** decrease mean arterial pressure
- **C)** decrease capillary hydrostatic pressure
- **D)** increase transcapillary fluid filtration
- **E)** increase blood flow through the capillary bed

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q234: When an organ responds to an increase in metabolic activity with a decrease in arteriolar resistance and increase of its blood flow, this is known as

- **A)** active hyperemia
- **B)** reactive hyperemia
- **C)** autoregulation of blood flow
- **D)** flow-dependent vasodilation
- **E)** metabolic vasoconstriction

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q235: A particular vascular bed demonstrates the phenomenon of autoregulation of blood flow. This means that

- **A)** when flow increases, capillary pressure increases
- **B)** when metabolic activity increases, flow increases
- **C)** when arterial pressure increases, arteriolar resistance increases
- **D)** when blood flow is interrupted, arteriolar resistance decreases
- **E)** when arterial pressure falls, sympathetic vasoconstriction occurs

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q236: Which of the following is most likely to increase coronary blood flow

- **A)** decreased arterial pressure
- **B)** decreased heart rate
- **C)** increased sympathetic neural activity
- **D)** reduced left ventricular end-diastolic volume
- **E)** reduced left ventricular ejection fraction

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q237: If arterial baroreceptors reflex is normal, a cardiovascular disturbance that decreases mean arterial pressure will causes a decrease in which of the followings

- **A)** urine output rate
- **B)** sympathetic nerve activity
- **C)** heart rate
- **D)** total peripheral resistance
- **E)** myocardial contractility

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q238: Which one of the following, after all reflex adjustments are complete, will result in an increase in mean arterial pressure?

- **A)** low carbon dioxide levels in arterial blood
- **B)** increased intracranial pressure
- **C)** decreased cardiac filling pressure
- **D)** low blood volume
- **E)** low oxygen levels in arterial blood

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q239: An individual has higher-than-normal mean arterial pressure and lower-than-normal pulse rate. Which of the following is most likely to cause such a combination?

- **A)** low oxygen levels in arterial blood
- **B)** anxiety
- **C)** exercise
- **D)** significant blood loss
- **E)** a drug that selectively stimulates alpha-adrenergic receptors

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q240: Which of the following is the primary ionic basis of prepotential of SA node?

- **A)** Ca ions influx through transient Ca channels
- **B)** Ca influx through long acting Ca channels
- **C)** Outward Na current
- **D)** Potassium efflux through leak channels

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q241: Normally the impulse the excites the left ventricular myocyte originates from which of the following?

- **A)** Purkinje system
- **B)** Left bundle branch
- **C)** SA node
- **D)** Ventricle

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q242: Conduction velocity is the slowest in which of the following?

- **A)** Atrial pathway
- **B)** Ventricular muscle
- **C)** SA node
- **D)** Purkinje fibres

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q243: What is the effect of vagal stimulation on the membrane potential of SA node?

- **A)** Increase inward calcium current
- **B)** It increases the slope of prepotential
- **C)** It activates a hyperpolarizing potassium current
- **D)** It increases intracellular cAMP

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q244: Activation of beta receptors in the heart is normally associated with which of the following?

- **A)** Decrease in the slope of phase 4 depolarization in SA nodal cells
- **B)** Increase uptake of Ca ions in sarcoplasmic reticulum by Ca ATPase
- **C)** Inhibition Ca induced Ca release following depolarization of in ventricular
- **D)** Decrease in conduction speed through AV node

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q245: Ability of AV node to generate its own impulses when SA node is sick is due to which of the following?

- **A)** A constant phase 4 membrane potential
- **B)** Slow calcium influx during phase 0
- **C)** Spontaneous diastolic depolarization
- **D)** Absence of prepotential

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q246: Propagation of repolarization from ventricular epicardium to endocardium is represented by which of the following?

- **A)** QRS complex
- **B)** T wave
- **C)** QT interval
- **D)** P wave

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q247: Electrical activity in which region of the heart does not result in deflection on the surface of electrocardiogram

- **A)** Atria
- **B)** Bundle of His
- **C)** Free wall left ventricle
- **D)** Free wall right ventricle
- **E)** Muscular portion of ventricular septum

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q248: In sinus rhythm the last portion of the ventricle to depolarize is which of the following?

- **A)** Interventricular septum from the right to the left
- **B)** Anteroseptal portion of myocardium
- **C)** Posterobasal portion of left ventricle and pulmonary conus
- **D)** Most of myocardium from endocardium to pericardium

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q249: The most reliable index of AV nodal delay is which of the following?

- **A)** P-R interval
- **B)** S-T segment
- **C)** Q-T interval
- **D)** P-R segment

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q250: The AV node does not conduct more than

- **A)** 180 impulse/min
- **B)** 300 impulse/min
- **C)** 230 impulse / min
- **D)** 330 impulse/min

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q251: During cardiac cycle, aortic valve closes at the end of

- **A)** Isometric contraction phase
- **B)** Diastasis
- **C)** Protodiastole
- **D)** Rapid ejection

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q252: The maximum pressure rise in the ventricle occurs during which of the following?

- **A)** Rapid ejection
- **B)** Protodiastole
- **C)** Diastasis
- **D)** Isometric contraction

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q253: In postnatal life, steady state outputs of right and left are matched in vivo by which of the following?

- **A)** Sympathetic effect on SA node
- **B)** Vagal influence on SA node
- **C)** Frank Starling's mechanism
- **D)** Matching tension generated by each ventricle
- **E)** Varying after load for each ventricle

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q254: What would be the change of blood flow to a tissue if radius of arteriole in that tissue is doubled and perfusion pressure is halved?

- **A)** Increase 16 times
- **B)** Increase 8 times
- **C)** Increase 12 times
- **D)** Decrease 4 times

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q255: Thin walled capillaries do not burst when intracapillary pressure is increased within physiological limits because which of the following?

- **A)** They lack smooth muscle in their walls
- **B)** Blood flow rate is low
- **C)** They have very small radius
- **D)** Capillary hematocrite is lower than whole body hematocrite

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q256: Which of the following is usually associated with turbulence of blood flow?

- **A)** Reynolds number less than 200
- **B)** Decrease of blood velocity
- **C)** Decreased of blood density
- **D)** Increase of diameter of blood vessels

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q257: Patients with acute cardiac failure may not have edema if

- **A)** Plasma Oncotic pressure is high
- **B)** Cardiac output is decreased
- **C)** Renal compensation occurs
- **D)** There is fall of systemic capillary pressure

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q258: Venous return is transiently increased during which of the following?

- **A)** Strain phase of Valsalva manoeuvre
- **B)** Deep expiration
- **C)** Severe capillary dilatation
- **D)** Deep inspiration

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q259: Quantitatively, the most important means of increasing blood flow to an actively metabolizing tissue is

- **A)** Increasing cardiac output
- **B)** Increasing blood pressure
- **C)** Decreasing local vascular resistance
- **D)** Increasing peripheral resistance

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q260: Which of the following are not vasodilator metabolites

- **A)** ADP
- **B)** Potassium ions
- **C)** H ions
- **D)** Endothelin

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q261: What is the chemical identity of endothelium derived relaxing factor?

- **A)** Nitric oxide
- **B)** Nitrous oxide
- **C)** Carbon monoxide
- **D)** Potassium

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q262: Which of the following has direct vasodilator effect on vascular smooth muscles in arterioles in the presence of endothelia dysfunction?

- **A)** Acetylcholine
- **B)** Nitric oxide
- **C)** Angiotensin II
- **D)** Thromboxane A2

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q263: Which of the following has vasoconstrictor effect?

- **A)** Angiotensin II
- **B)** Nitric oxide
- **C)** Bradykinin
- **D)** Histamine

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q264: Blood flow to exercising muscles is decreased by which of the following?

- **A)** Accumulation of K+
- **B)** Adenosine
- **C)** Increased PCO2 in muscles
- **D)** Norepinephrine
- **E)** Activation of sympathetic cholinergic vasodilator system

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q265: The most potent vasoconstrictor is which of the following?

- **A)** Angiotensin II
- **B)** Vasopressin
- **C)** Endothelin I
- **D)** Noradrenaline

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q266: On sudden rising from supine position which of the following occurs?

- **A)** Central blood volume increase
- **B)** Central venous pressure increase
- **C)** Heart rate decreases
- **D)** Discharge form arterial baroreceptors decreases

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q267: Intravenous injection of noradrenaline to a normotensive healthy adult human leads to

- **A)** An increase of ABP and HR
- **B)** A decrease in ABP and HR
- **C)** A decrease in ABP and increased in HR
- **D)** An increase in ABP and decrease in HR

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q268: During strain phase of Valsalva maneuver (forced expiration with the glottis closed and maintaining expiratory pressure at 40 mmHg for 15 second) which of the following is incorrect?

- **A)** Venous return decreases
- **B)** Cardiac output decreases
- **C)** ABP decreases
- **D)** Heart rate decreases
- **E)** Sympathetic outflow to blood vessels increase

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q269: The first reflex response to an increase in arterial pressure produced by intravenous injection of phenylephrine is

- **A)** Increase in heart rate
- **B)** Increase in cardiac out put
- **C)** Increase in P-R interval of ECG
- **D)** Increased peripheral resistance

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q270: In hemorrhaged dogs with marked hypotension (mean arterial pressure < 50 mmHg) denervation of arterial chemoreceptors would:

- **A)** Increase ABP since chemoreceptors reduce sympathetic outflow
- **B)** Result in a further drop of ABP as arterial chemoreflex is sympathoexcitatory
- **C)** Produce no change since arterial chemoreceptors do not influence sympathetic outflow
- **D)** Depend on whether arterial baroreceptors are reset or not

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q271: The acute effect of bilateral clamping of carotid arteries proximal to carotid sinus is

- **A)** An increase in heart rate and mean blood pressure
- **B)** A decrease in heart rate and mean blood pressure
- **C)** A decrease in heart rate and increase in mean blood pressure
- **D)** An increase in heart rate and a decrease in mean blood pressure

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q272: At which of the following areas blood brain barrier is leaky?

- **A)** Organum vasculosum of lamina terminalis
- **B)** Subfornical organ
- **C)** Posterior pituitary gland
- **D)** Anterior pituitary gland
- **E)** Area postrema

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q273: If the noradrenergic nerves to the heart imulated after giving beta blocker, then what would be the effect on coronary blood flow?

- **A)** Vasoconstriction
- **B)** Vasodilatation
- **C)** No change
- **D)** Unpredictable

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q274: Normally left ventricle is more vulnerable for ischemia and infarction compared to right ventricle because

- **A)** Pulmonary vascular resistance is more than systemic vascular resistance
- **B)** Left ventricle pumps more blood than right ventricle
- **C)** Left ventricular subendocardial perfusion occur during ventricular diastole
- **D)** Diastolic pressure is equivalent in both ventricles

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q275: The wheal in the triple response is due to

- **A)** Contraction of precapillary sphincter
- **B)** Increased capillary permeability
- **C)** Axon reflex
- **D)** Decreased absorption of fluid

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q276: Which of the following physiologic responses is due to local axon reflex?

- **A)** Flare
- **B)** Red line
- **C)** White reaction
- **D)** Reactive hyperemia

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q277: A 20 years old patient was subjected to surgery, then about 3 litres of intravenous fluid was given within one hour, this led to increase in stroke volume and increase in cardiac output. In this patient, the strength of contraction of left ventricular muscle increased due to....

- **A)** Blood calcium levels fall
- **B)** End-diastolic ventricular filling volume rises
- **C)** Serum potassium levels rise
- **D)** Strenuous exercise is undertaken
- **E)** Peripheral resistance is increased

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q278: Which one of the following will increase if massaging the neck the carotid sinus baroreceptor?

- **A)** Total peripheral resistance
- **B)** Right atrial pressure
- **C)** Venous tone
- **D)** Ventricular contractility
- **E)** Vagal nerve activity

**Correct Answer:** E

# Source File: 26_Dept_Physiology_MCQ_2026.md

### Q1: Which of the following best defines \"automaticity\" in cardiac physiology?

- **A)** The ability to maintain blood pressure
- **B)** The ability to contract without neural input
- **C)** The ability to rhythmically depolarize with external stimulation
- **D)** The ability to generate action potentials spontaneously without external stimuli
- **E)** The ability to conduct impulses rapidly through cardiac muscle

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q2: Which of the following statements is TRUE regarding cardiac pacemaker hierarchy?

- **A)** AV node normally overrides SA node
- **B)** All cardiac cells contract at the same rhythm
- **C)** Purkinje fibers initiate the heart's impulse under normal conditions
- **D)** SA node is the primary pacemaker due to its fastest rate of discharge
- **E)** AV node has a faster rate than the SA node

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q3: If the SA node fails, which structure typically takes over as the pacemaker?

- **A)** Atrial muscle
- **B)** Purkinje fibers
- **C)** Mitral valve
- **D)** AV node
- **E)** Ventricular septum

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q4: Which of the following is the correct intrinsic firing rate of the Purkinje fibers?

- **A)** 60-100 bpm
- **B)** 45-60 bpm
- **C)** 70-85 bpm
- **D)** 100-110 bpm
- **E)** 25-40 bpm

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q5: Which of the following statements is FALSE?

- **A)** The AV node has a slower intrinsic rate than the SA node
- **B)** All parts of the cardiac conduction system have Pacemaker potential
- **C)** Contractile cardiac muscle cells typically initiate action potentials
- **D)** The SA node initiates impulses at 100-110 bpm under intrinsic conditions
- **E)** The heart can contract independently of nervous input

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q6: A 65-year-old man is brought to the emergency department after experiencing dizziness and fatigue. His ECG reveals an absence of P waves and a regular ventricular rhythm at a rate of 45 beats per minute. Further evaluation shows That the sinoatrial (SA) node is nonfunctional. Which of the following is the most likely pacemaker responsible for maintaining this patient's heart rate?

- **A)** SA node
- **B)** Atrial muscle fibers
- **C)** AV node
- **D)** Purkinje fibers
- **E)** Ventricular myocardium

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q7: A 72-year-old woman presents with syncope. Her ECG shows a ventricular rhythm of 30 beats per minute with no P waves or QRS complexes originating from above the ventricles. The diagnosis of complete heart block is made, with failure of impulse conduction from the atria to the ventricles. Which of the following statements best explains the origin of the cardiac rhythm in this patient?

- **A)** The SA node is generating impulses at 100-110 bpm
- **B)** The AV node is pacing the heart at 60 bpm
- **C)** The Purkinje fibers are acting as the pacemaker
- **D)** Contractile cells in the atrial wall are initiating the rhythm
- **E)** The vagus nerve is initiating action potentials in the ventricles

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q8: Which of the following best supports the evidence that cardiac autorhythmicity is a myogenic property?

- **A)** Presence of vagal innervation to the heart
- **B)** The heart stops beating when denervated
- **C)** Denervated hearts continue to beat
- **D)** Opening of L-type Ca++ channels
- **E)** Na+-K+ pump activity

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q9: What is the term used for spontaneous, gradual depolarization of SA node cells during early diastole?

- **A)** Afterdepolarization
- **B)** Plateau potential
- **C)** Prepotential or pacemaker potential
- **D)** Overshoot potential

- **C)** Q wave starts before and R, S waves occur during it
- **D)** T wave
- **E)** U wave

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q176: The a-wave of the jugular venous pulse corresponds to which of the following?

- **A)** Closure of the AV valves
- **B)** Ejection of blood into the aorta
- **C)** Atrial relaxation
- **D)** Atrial contraction
- **E)** Ventricular filling

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q177: Which valves are open during the rapid ejection phase of the cardiac cycle?

- **A)** Both AV and semilunar valves
- **B)** Only AV valves
- **C)** Only semilunar valves
- **D)** All valves are closed
- **E)** Only tricuspid valve

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q178: Which of the following best describes the type of muscle contraction during the rapid ejection phase?

- **A)** Isometric contraction
- **B)** Isotonic contraction with muscle shortening
- **C)** Tonic contraction without shortening
- **D)** Partial contraction with relaxation
- **E)** Eccentric contraction

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q179: What is the approximate peak pressure in the left ventricle during rapid ejection?

- **A)** 80 mmHg
- **B)** 100 mmHg
- **C)** 60 mmHg
- **D)** 120 mmHg
- **E)** 25 mmHg

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q180: During the rapid ejection phase, which of the following happens to the aortic pressure?

- **A)** Falls rapidly
- **B)** Remains constant
- **C)** Drops to diastolic level
- **D)** Rises to systolic peak (~120 mmHg)
- **E)** Equals right atrial pressure

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q181: Which heart sound component is associated with the rapid ejection of blood?

- **A)** First component of S1
- **B)** Second heart sound (S2)
- **C)** Third heart sound (S3)
- **D)** Second component of first heart sound (S1)
- **E)** Fourth heart sound (S4)

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q182: What happens to atrial pressure at the beginning of the rapid ejection phase?

- **A)** Increases due to atrial systole
- **B)** Decreases due to descent of AV ring
- **C)** Increases due to AV valve closure
- **D)** Remains constant
- **E)** Rises due to pulmonary congestion

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q183: The pressure in the pulmonary artery during the rapid ejection phase reaches approximately:

- **A)** 10 mmHg
- **B)** 25 mmHg
- **C)** 40 mmHg
- **D)** 80 mmHg
- **E)** 5 mmHg

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q184: A 25-year-old male undergoes a routine echocardiogram, which shows normal ventricular contraction. During one phase of the cardiac cycle, the semilunar valves are open, AV valves are closed, and blood is rapidly ejected into the aorta and pulmonary artery. At the same time, aortic pressure is rising. Which of the following best describes this phase?

- **A)** Isovolumetric contraction
- **B)** Rapid ejection phase
- **C)** Isovolumetric relaxation
- **D)** Atrial systole
- **E)** Reduced ejection phase

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q185: During cardiac catheterization of a healthy adult, measurements show the following values in one phase: Left ventricular pressure: 120 mmHg, Pulmonary artery pressure: 25 mmHg, Aortic pressure: increasing, Atrial pressure: initially falls then rises slightly and ECG shows the end of the S-T segment and start of T wave. Which of the following events is most likely occurring during this phase?

- **A)** AV valves are open and blood is entering ventricles
- **B)** Semilunar valves are closed and no volume change occurs
- **C)** Blood is rapidly leaving the ventricles into the arteries
- **D)** Ventricles are filling passively
- **E)** Ventricular pressure is lower than atrial pressure

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q186: A 30-year-old woman undergoes a cardiovascular stress test. During rapid ventricular contraction, blood is forcefully ejected into the arterial system. Her physician notes a transient drop in atrial pressure and ventricular volume is also observed to drop rapidly. Which of the following is the cause of transient drop of atrial pressure?

- **A)** Bulging of AV cusps into atrial cavity
- **B)** Descent of AV ring into ventricle increasing atrial capcity
- **C)** AV valve closure
- **D)** Venous return to the atria
- **E)** Atrial relaxation

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q187: Which of the following best describes the state of cardiac valves during most of the protodiastolic phase?

- **A)** AV valves and semilunar valves are both open
- **B)** All valves are closed
- **C)** Semilunar valves are open, AV valves are closed
- **D)** Semilunar valves are closed, AV valves are open
- **E)** Both AV and semilunar valves are open

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q188: What causes the dicrotic notch on the aortic pressure curve during the cardiac cycle?

- **A)** Closure of AV valves
- **B)** Rapid ejection of blood into the aorta
- **C)** Isovolumetric contraction
- **D)** Backflow of blood causing closure of the aortic valve
- **E)** Atrial contraction

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q189: A 30-year-old healthy man undergoes a cardiac catheterization. The pressure tracing from his aorta shows a small downward deflection followed by a rebound upstroke immediately after the T wave. This is most likely due to:

- **A)** Opening of the AV valves
- **B)** Isovolumetric relaxation
- **C)** Closure of the pulmonary valve
- **D)** Closure of the aortic valve with dicrotic notch formation
- **E)** Rapid ventricular filling

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q190: Which of the following best describes the valve status during isovolumetric relaxation?

- **A)** All valves are open
- **B)** AV valves are open, semilunar valves are closed
- **C)** AV valves are closed, semilunar valves are open
- **D)** Only AV valves are open
- **E)** All valves are closed

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q191: What happens to ventricular volume during isovolumetric relaxation?

- **A)** It increases rapidly
- **B)** It decreases rapidly
- **C)** It stay's constant
- **D)** It varies with heart rate
- **E)** It becomes zero

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q192: What causes the dicrotic wave on the aortic pressure curve during isovolumetric relaxation?

- **A)** Opening of semilunar valves
- **B)** Recoil of aortic wall and rebound of blood after valve closure
- **C)** Ventricular contraction
- **D)** Atrial contraction
- **E)** Sudden closure of AV valves

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q193: At Which of the following cardiac cycle phases, second heart sound occurs?

- **A)** Atrial systole
- **B)** Isometric contraction phase
- **C)** Maximum ejection phase
- **D)** Isometric relaxation phase
- **E)** Rapid filling phase

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q194: A 22-year-old medical student hears the second heart sound while examining a classmate. What cardiac event corresponds with this sound?

- **A)** Opening of mitral valve
- **B)** Opening of aortic valve
- **C)** Closure of tricuspid valve
- **D)** Closure of semilunar valves during isovolumetric relaxation
- **E)** Ventricular ejection

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q195: A patient undergoes cardiac catheterization. A phase is recorded in which ventricular pressure drops rapidly, volume remains constant, and aortic pressure shows a rebound wave. Which of the following is correct regarding this phase?

- **A)** Ventricular filling is occurring
- **B)** Semilunar valves are open
- **C)** This is the isovolumetric relaxation phase with dicrotic wave formation
- **D)** Ventricles are contracting
- **E)** Atrial systole is occurring

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q196: During a physiology experiment, the instructor mentions a phase with no change in ventricular volume, closure of semilunar valves, and a rapid drop in ventricular pressure. Which event follows next in a normal cardiac cycle?

- **A)** Rapid ejection
- **B)** Ventricular systole
- **C)** Atrial systole
- **D)** AV valve opening and rapid ventricular filling
- **E)** Ventricular depolarization

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q197: During the rapid filling phase of the cardiac cycle, which of the following valves are open?

- **A)** Both AV and semilunar valves
- **B)** AV valves only
- **C)** Semilunar valves only
- **D)** All valves
- **E)** All valves are closed

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q198: The third heart sound is best associated with which of the following?

- **A)** Closure of AV valves
- **B)** Ventricular systole
- **C)** Rapid filling of the ventricles
- **D)** Atrial systole
- **E)** Isovolumetric contraction

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q199: What happens to atrial pressure during the rapid filling phase?

- **A)** Remains constant
- **B)** Increases due to atrial contraction
- **C)** Increases due to blood accumulation
- **D)** Decreases due to rapid outflow to ventricles
- **E)** Fluctuates randomly

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q200: A 25-year-old male is undergoing echocardiography. The technician observes a rapid increase in ventricular volume with minimal atrial pressure and a third heart sound. Which phase of the cardiac cycle is most likely being observed?

- **A)** Isovolumetric contraction
- **B)** Rapid filling
- **C)** Reduced filling
- **D)** Atrial systole
- **E)** Ventricular ejection

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q201: A healthy volunteer participates in a cardiac physiology experiment. During one phase, the AV valves are open, ventricular volume increases slowly, and no heart sounds are heard. Which phase is this?

- **A)** Isovolumetric relaxation
- **B)** Reduced filling
- **C)** Rapid filling
- **D)** Atrial systole
- **E)** Ventricular systole

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q202: Which of the following best describes ventricular volume during the reduced filling phase?

- **A)** Decreases slowly
- **B)** Increases slowly
- **C)** Increases rapidly
- **D)** Remains constant
- **E)** Becomes equal to atrial volume

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q203: Which of the following best describes diastasis during the cardiac cycle?

- **A)** Rapid ventricular filling due to high atrial pressure
- **B)** Active filling of ventricles by atrial systole
- **C)** Passive and very slow ventricular filling before atrial systole
- **D)** Isometric contraction of ventricles
- **E)** Closure of all heart valves

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q204: How does diastasis change with increasing heart rate?

- **A)** It increases in duration
- **B)** It remains unchanged
- **C)** It disappears at very low heart rates
- **D)** It progressively shortens and may disappear at high heart rates
- **E)** It increases due to higher venous return

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q205: At what heart rate does diastasis usually disappear without significantly affecting the ventricular filling volume?

- **A)** 75 bpm
- **B)** 90 bpm
- **C)** 100 bpm
- **D)** 125 bpm
- **E)** 180 bpm

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q206: Which of the following correctly represents Lead I in a standard bipolar limb ECG?

- **A)** LL-RA
- **B)** LL - LA
- **C)** LA-RA
- **D)** RA - LA

**Correct Answer:** C

- **D)** 1.0 mV
- **E)** 1.2 mV

- **B)** LA (-) to LL (+)

- **D)** LL (-) to LA (+)
- **E)** RA (-) to RA (+)

- **C)** 0.6 mV
- **D)** 0.8 mV
- **E)** 1.9 mV

- **D)** aVR

- **C)** Left ventricle
- **D)** Right ventricle
- **E)** Sinoatrial node

- **D)** Left bundle branch block

- **D)** V3

### Q219: Why does each ECG lead record a different appearance of the depolarization wave?

- **B)** Because of differing electrode materials
- **C)** Because the depolarization and repolarization waves spreads in different directions relative to the +ve electrode
- **D)** Because heart rate changes from lead to lead

### Q220: Why is the T wave typically upright (positive) in most ECG leads, despite being a repolarization wave?

- **B)** Because atrial repolarization is very prominent
- **C)** Because repolarization occurs in the same direction as depolarization
- **D)** Because ventricular repolarization occurs from epicardium to endocardium
- **E)** Because atrial depolarization and repolarization cancel each other

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q221: Which of the following statements about atrial repolarization is true?

- **B)** It is responsible for the T wave
- **C)** It is usually not visible on surface ECG because it is masked by the QRS complex
- **D)** It generates a large positive wave on all leads
- **E)** It contributes to the PR interval

**Correct Answer:** C

- **B)** R wave
- **C)** P wave

- **D)** 0.1 mV
- **E)** 2.0 mV

- **B)** Depolarization of the ventricles

- **E)** Conduction through the AV node

- **E)** 0.20 sec

- **D)** The left ventricle repolarizes before the right

- **B)** 0.05-0.10 sec

- **E)** 0.20-0.30 sec

### Q234: A 70-year-old man presents with dizziness and fatigue. ECG shows a prolonged PR interval, but all P waves are followed by QRS complexes. Which of the following is the most likely cause of this ECG finding?

### Q263: A 58-year-old man presents with syncope. His ECG shows complete heart block. His JVP shows intermittent giant a waves not followed by c or v waves. What are these waves called?

- **B)** c waves

- **D)** Cannon waves
- **E)** False waves

### Q264: A patient is suspected to have AV nodal block. The a-c interval on the JVP tracing is prolonged >0.21 sec. What degree of heart block is this?

- **B)** Second-degree AV block
- **C)** Third-degree AV block
- **D)** Sinoatrial block
- **E)** Bundle branch block

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q265: A 52-year-old male has 2:1 AV block. Which JVP pattern is expected?

- **D)** Irregular v waves
- **E)** Multiple c waves

### Q266: A 60-year-old man has been diagnosed with congestive right heart failure. Which JVP finding is expected?

- **D)** Absent v waves
- **E)** Flattened y descent

### Q267: Which of the following statements about the Cardiac Inhibitory Center (CIC) is correct?

- **B)** Sends sympathetic fibers directly to the heart
- **C)** Inhibits the heart via the vagus nerve
- **D)** Located in the pons
- **E)** Stimulates adrenal medulla secretion

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q268: The vasoconstrictor center (C1) exerts its effects by which of the following?

- **A)** Activating parasympathetic fibers
- **B)** Directly releasing norepinephrine
- **C)** Stimulating lower lumbar spinal neurons
- **D)** Increasing sympathetic outflow to blood vessels and heart
- **E)** Releasing acetylcholine at effector organs

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q269: Which of the following is a function of the vasodilator center (A1) in the medulla?

- **B)** Directly innervates vascular smooth muscle
- **C)** Inhibits the vasoconstrictor center to cause vasodilation
- **D)** Stimulates CIC to increase heart rate
- **E)** Increases blood pressure during hemorrhage

### Q270: A 55-year-old man presents with severe hypotension after blood loss due to trauma. His heart rate is 120 bpm, and peripheral vasoconstriction is observed. Which of the following centers is most likely highly active?

- **B)** Vasodilator center (A1)
- **C)** Vasoconstrictor center (C1)
- **D)** Sensory cortex
- **E)** Nucleus tractus solitarius

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q271: A researcher stimulates a region in the medulla oblongata of an experimental animal. As a result, heart rate decreases significantly without affecting vasoconstriction. Which area was most likely stimulated?

- **C)** Vasodilator center (A1)
- **D)** Sensory area (A2)
- **E)** Adrenal medulla

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q272: A 30-year-old athlete has a resting heart rate of 55 bpm. His ECG is otherwise normal. Which of the following best explains his low resting heart rate?

### Q273: A lab animal's heart stops following intense vagal stimulation but resumes beating at a rate of 30 bpm. What is this phenomenon called?

### Q281: Which of the following is not caused by Vagal stimulation to the heart?

### Q283: A 35-year-old athlete is administered a drug that blocks sympathetic Î²1-adrenergic receptors. Which of the following is the most likely immediate cardiac effect?

### Q285: Sympathetic tone to the heart at rest results in which of the following?

### Q286: A 22-year-old medical student experiences a rapid increase in heart rate before entering the examination hall. Which of the following best explains this phenomenon?

### Q349: Which of the following best represents the concept of preload in cardiac physiology?

- **B)** The resistance to blood flow in the peripheral arteries
- **C)** The volume of blood remaining in the ventricle after systole
- **D)** The volume of blood in the ventricle at the end of diastole
- **E)** The contractile strength of cardiac muscle fibers independent of stretch

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q350: According to Frank-Starling's law, an increase in venous return will lead to which of the following?

- **A)** Decreased myocardial oxygen consumption
- **B)** Decreased cardiac contractility
- **C)** Increased SV due to greater fiber stretch
- **D)** Decreased end-diastolic volume
- **E)** Decreased heart rate via baroreceptor reflex

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q351: A patient with severe hemorrhage is noted to have reduced venous return. Which of the following changes is most likely observed as a result?

- **A)** Increased end-diastolic volume
- **B)** Increased preload
- **C)** Decreased stroke volume
- **D)** Increased cardiac output
- **E)** Reflex increase in preload due to Frank-Starling mechanism

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q352: A 24-year-old athlete is running on a treadmill. During vigorous exercise, his skeletal muscle contractions increase. Which of the following best explains how this affects venous return?

- **A)** Increased resistance to venous return
- **B)** Decreased capillary tone
- **C)** Skeletal muscle pump enhances venous return
- **D)** Decreased mean systemic filling pressure
- **E)** Decreased blood flow to the heart

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q353: During a forced expiration (e.g., Valsalva maneuver), which of the following is most likely to occur?

- **B)** VR decreases due to +ve intrathoracic pressure
- **C)** VR remains unchanged
- **D)** MSFP increases
- **E)** CVP decreases

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q354: A 40-year-old woman receives an intravenous infusion of a large volume of saline. How does this affect mean systemic filling pressure and venous return?

- **B)** MSFP increases; VR decreases
- **C)** MSFP increases; VR increases
- **D)** MSFP decreases; VR increases
- **E)** Both remain unchanged

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q355: In a person who stands up suddenly from a supine position, which of the following is likely?

- **D)** Cardiac output initially rises

### Q356: A patient suffers a spinal cord injury that impairs sympathetic tone. Which effect on venous return is most expected?

- **B)** Decrease in VR due to loss of venous tone

- **D)** Increased MSFP

### Q357: A patient experiences a severe anaphylactic reaction leading to histamine release. How does this affect venous return?

- **C)** Blood pools in capillaries, reducing VR

### Q358: During inspiration, what combination of intrathoracic and intra-abdominal pressure changes enhances VR?

### Q359: An elderly patient presents with chronic leg swelling and visible varicose veins. On examination, there is no sign of cardiac or renal dysfunction. Which of the following is the most likely cause of impaired venous return in this condition?

- **B)** Increased resistance in arterioles

- **D)** Excessively contracted skeletal muscles
- **E)** Reduced pressure in the right atrium

### Q360: A healthy individual takes deep, rhythmic breaths. Which factor primarily promotes increased venous return during inspiration?

- **E)** Capillary vasodilation

### Q361: A 28-year-old man is dehydrated after prolonged fasting. What will be the effect on VR?

- **D)** Increased VR due to vasodilation
- **E)** No change

### Q362: A patient receives beta-adrenergic agonist therapy. Which of the following will occur in the venous system?

- **B)** Venoconstriction and increased MSFP

### Q363: Which change would most likely increase VR during prolonged exercise?

- **B)** Increased arteriolar constriction in active muscles
- **C)** Increased skeletal muscle pump action
- **D)** Increased venous pooling

### Q364: Which of the following explains why cardiac suction during systole increases VR?

### Q366: Which of the following pressures drives venous return to the heart?

### Q367: Venous return is inversely related to which of the following?

### Q368: Which of the following correctly represents the formula for venous return?

### Q375: A persistent increase in afterload due to long-standing hypertension is most likely to cause which of the following?

### Q376: A 62-year-old man with a long history of uncontrolled hypertension presents with signs of left ventricular hypertrophy. Which of the following best explains his condition?

### Q379: A 70-year-old woman with decompensated heart failure shows high end-diastolic volume but low stroke volume. Which mechanism best explains this paradox?

### Q380: A 56-year-old man has elevated arterial blood pressure and a preserved ejection fraction. Over time, he is at risk of which of the following?

### Q381: A 25-year-old athlete has an increased venous return during strenuous exercise. Which of the following mechanisms allows his heart to accommodate the increased preload without a change in heart rate?

### Q384: A patient has a sudden rise in arterial blood pressure. Within 5 minutes, his stroke volume remains high but the end-diastolic volume returns to baseline. What mechanism explains this observation?

### Q385: A young man has increased right atrial pressure due to rapid IV fluid infusion. Which of the following changes is most likely observed within 30 seconds?

### Q386: Which of the following statements correctly describes heterometric regulation?

### Q387: Which of the following best describes homeometric regulation?

### Q388: Which of the following is NOT a feature of heterometric regulation?

### Q389: Which of the following contributes to homeometric regulation of cardiac output?

### Q390: A 24-year-old athlete runs a marathon. During the race, his venous return increases significantly. Which mechanism ensures that his heart pumps the extra returned blood effectively without a rise in heart rate?

### Q391: Following a transfusion, a patient's venous return increases abruptly. Which of the following will initially increase as a result of heterometric autoregulation?

### Q392: During acute exercise, the heart increases its cardiac output without a significant increase in EDV after a few beats. This persistent increase in contractility and stroke volume is explained by:

- **B)** Homeometric autoregulation

- **D)** Anrep's effect

- **B)** It causes persistent rise in EDV

### Q395: Which of the following is NOT a feature of heterometric regulation?

### Q516: In a patient with acute blood loss, the arterial pressure drops below 60 mmHg. Which reflex pathway becomes active as a result of chemoreceptor stimulation?

- **A)** Inhibition of vasoconstrictor center
- **B)** Activation of cardioinhibitory center
- **C)** Activation of VVC and inhibition of CIC
- **D)** Stimulation of vagal tone
- **E)** Stimulation of parasympathetic outflow

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q517: A patient with chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) has elevated arterial CO2 and low oxygen levels. Which cardiovascular response is expected due to arterial chemoreceptor activation?

- **B)** Decreased cardiac output
- **C)** Increased sympathetic tone and heart rate
- **D)** Parasympathetic inhibition of the heart
- **E)** Decreased catecholamine release

### Q518: A patient with bilateral carotid body resection experiences impaired response to hypoxia. Which of the following responses is most likely reduced?

### Q519: During high-altitude exposure, arterial oxygen levels fall and ABP. Which of the following contributes to elevating arterial blood pressure in response to this hypoxic state?

### Q520: A 24-year-old male is brought to the emergency department after a motorcycle accident. On arrival, he is pale, confused, and sweating profusely. ### Q521: What is the main effector mechanism through which arterial chemoreflex elevates blood pressure?

### Q523: A patient presents with a massive hemorrhage leading to a mean ABP of 45 mmHg. Which of the following mechanisms is likely to become active at this stage to restore blood pressure?

### Q524: In a case of severe hypotension, the CNS ischemic response is triggered. Which of the following best describes its primary effector action?

### Q525: A patient with a brain tumor develops elevated intracranial pressure. His blood pressure spikes to 180/100 mmHg, and his heart rate drops to 48 bpm. What is the most likely explanation?

### Q526: A neurosurgery patient presents with increased intracranial pressure and a triad of hypertension, bradycardia, and irregular respiration. This clinical picture is typical of:

### Q527: Which of the following best describes the CNS ischemic response?

### Q528: Which of the following statements about Cushing's reaction is TRUE?

### Q532: A patient is given an intravenous fluid bolus, leading to a transient increase in blood volume and arterial blood pressure. Which of the following mechanisms helps restore blood pressure back toward normal over the next few hours?

### Q535: A man received an infusion of 2 liters of normal saline that increases venous return and distension of the right atrium associated with an increase in ABP. Which of the following reflexes is likely to activated and its effects to restore ABP to its normal level?

### Q767: A healthy individual's cardiac O2 consumption rises from 27 ml/min at rest to 152 ml/min during maximal exertion. What is the term used to describe the heart's capacity to make this adjustment?

- **A)** Stroke volume reserve
- **B)** Coronary reserve
- **C)** Oxygen debt
- **D)** Pulmonary reserve
- **E)** Preload reserve

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q768: Which of the following is a primary function of pulmonary circulation?

- **A)** Storage of oxygen in alveoli
- **B)** Arterialization of systemic arterial blood
- **C)** Arterialization of venous blood
- **D)** Exchange of nutrients in bronchial tissues
- **E)** Cooling of body temperature

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q769: One of the protective roles of pulmonary circulation is to:

- **A)** Prevent water loss by filtration
- **B)** Filter arterial blood from pathogens
- **C)** Filter venous blood from clots, fat, and air emboli
- **D)** Secrete surfactant to reduce surface tension
- **E)** Activate vitamin D for bone metabolism

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q770: Which of the following best describes the pulmonary circulation?

- **A)** High-pressure, low-flow
- **B)** Low-pressure, low-flow
- **C)** Low-pressure, high-flow
- **D)** High-pressure, high-flow
- **E)** No-pressure, high-volume

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q771: What is the approximate mean pressure in the pulmonary artery?

- **E)** 35 mmHg

### Q772: Which feature of pulmonary arterioles contributes to their lower peripheral resistance?

- **D)** Less smooth muscle than systemic arterioles
- **E)** Large tunica adventitia

### Q773: The bronchial circulation provides blood supply to all of the following EXCEPT one. What is this exception?

- **E)** Pulmonary artery adventitia

### Q774: Which of the following is TRUE about pulmonary capillaries compared to systemic capillaries?

- **B)** Pulmonary capillaries are less permeable
- **C)** Pulmonary capillaries have shorter exchange surface
- **D)** Pulmonary capillaries have slower flow
- **E)** Pulmonary capillaries have higher permeability

### Q775: During rest, what is the approximate velocity of blood flow through the pulmonary capillaries?

- **E)** 2.0 seconds

### Q776: Which of the following best describes the pulmonary vascular response to hypoxia?

### Q777: The difference between left and right ventricular cardiac output due to bronchial venous drainage is approximately:

- **E)** Zero

### Q778: Which of the following is a distinguishing feature of pulmonary circulation?

### Q779: Which of the following hormones causes vasoconstriction in the pulmonary circulation?

### Q780: A 28-year-old athlete is undergoing intense physical training. During exercise, her cardiac output increases significantly. Which of the following best explains how her pulmonary circulation accommodates this increase without a significant rise in pulmonary arterial pressure?

- **B)** Passive constriction of pulmonary vessels

- **D)** Passive dilatation of pulmonary vessels
- **E)** Decreased heart rate

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q781: A patient with chronic bronchitis presents with low alveolar oxygen tension. Which vascular response helps maintain efficient gas exchange in such a situation?

- **B)** Vasoconstriction of vessels around well-ventilated alveoli
- **C)** Vasoconstriction of vessels around poorly ventilated alveoli
- **D)** General vasodilation of all pulmonary vessels
- **E)** Increase in bronchial circulation

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q782: In a patient lying flat (recumbent), pulmonary blood flow is uniform. However, upon standing, he experiences slight dizziness. Which of the following explains the reduced blood flow to the apex of his lungs?

- **C)** Increase in alveolar pressure at the base
- **D)** Equalization of arterial and venous pressure
- **E)** Vasoconstriction due to elevated CO2

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q783: A mountain climber experiences low oxygen levels (hypoxia) at high altitudes. Which of the following changes is expected in the pulmonary vasculature in response to alveolar hypoxia?

- **B)** Increased systemic vascular resistance
- **C)** Vasoconstriction in hypoxic lung regions
- **D)** Increased bronchial blood flow
- **E)** Decreased capillary permeability

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q784: Which factor plays a passive role in regulating pulmonary blood flow during exercise?

### Q785: What is the effect of decreased alveolar oxygen tension (e.g., below 75 mmHg) on pulmonary arterioles?

### Q786: A patient is undergoing surgery under general anesthesia. During mechanical ventilation, his intrapleural pressure becomes less negative. What is the most likely effect on pulmonary vascular resistance?

- **D)** It will decrease due to decreased sympathetic tone

### Q787: A mountain climber experiences shortness of breath and increased pulmonary pressure at high altitude. What is the most likely cause of the elevated pulmonary pressure?

- **D)** Decreased respiratory rate

### Q788: A 24-year-old athlete is undergoing an intense treadmill test. His cardiac output increases fourfold, yet his pulmonary arterial pressure only rises slightly. Which of the following mechanisms best explains this?

### Q789: During quiet inspiration, the intrapleural pressure becomes more negative. This leads to which of the following changes in pulmonary vessels?

- **D)** Compression of pulmonary veins

### Q790: Which of the following chemical substances causes vasodilation of pulmonary vessels and decreases pulmonary arterial pressure?

### Q791: Sympathetic stimulation of the pulmonary arterioles leads to which of the following effects?

### Q792: Which of the following best explains why pulmonary arterial pressure does not rise significantly during strenuous exercise?

### Q793: A 65-year-old patient in hypovolemic shock has a significantly reduced pulmonary arterial pressure. Which zone of pulmonary blood flow is most likely to be observed in the upper lung fields?

### Q794: A patient with mitral stenosis presents with pulmonary edema. Which of the following most likely contributed to fluid accumulation in the alveoli?

### Q795: A healthy person standing upright is examined for pulmonary blood flow distribution. Which pattern is expected in the lower lobes of the lungs?

### Q796: What is the role of negative interstitial fluid pressure in keeping alveoli dry?

### Q799: What is the typical resting oxygen consumption in a healthy adult at rest?

### Q800: A 25-year-old trained athlete performs a high-intensity cycling workout. During the session, his muscles exhibit significantly increased oxygen uptake and blood flow. Which of the following most likely contributes to this physiologic response in active muscles?

### Q801: Which of the following mechanisms does not contribute to the increased venous return during exercise?

### Q802: A 25-year-old runner begins intense physical activity. His venous return increases significantly. Which of the following contributes most directly to this increase during muscle contraction?

- **D)** Increased plasma oncotic pressure
- **E)** Decreased intrathoracic pressure

### Q803: A 30-year-old cyclist experiences a rapid increase in cardiac preload during vigorous training. Which of the following is least likely to be responsible for this rise in venous return?

### Q804: Which of the following best explains the rise in systolic blood pressure during exercise?

### Q805: Why does diastolic blood pressure typically remain unchanged or decrease slightly during dynamic exercise?

### Q806: A 28-year-old athlete shows a systolic pressure of 210 mmHg and unchanged diastolic pressure during intense cycling. Which of the following best explains the unchanged diastolic pressure?

### Q807: A patient performs prolonged endurance exercise. He later develops transient albuminuria and reduced urine output. What is the most likely underlying cause?

### Q809: Which of the following occurs in the skeletal muscle capillaries during vigorous exercise?

### Q815: Which of the following is primarily responsible for the increase in arterio-venous oxygen difference during exercise?

### Q816: A 23-year-old athlete performs intense cycling exercise. Blood analysis shows an arterio-venous oxygen difference of 15 mL%. Which of the following best explains this physiological change?

### Q817: During a treadmill test, a 21-year-old man has increased COâ, temperature, and Hâº concentration in his leg muscles. Which of the following best describes the resulting change in oxygen delivery to those muscles?

### Q818: A 28-year-old male is brought to the emergency room after a motorcycle accident. He is conscious but pale, with rapid heart rate and low blood pressure. Which of the following is the most immediate compensatory mechanism?

- **A)** Increase in erythropoietin secretion
- **B)** Fluid shift from interstitial space to blood
- **C)** Increased red blood cell production
- **D)** Decreased sympathetic outflow

### Q819: A patient develops acute blood loss during surgery. Shortly after, sympathetic stimulation causes vasoconstriction. What is the main goal of this response?

- **A)** Reduce cardiac workload
- **B)** Increase capillary hydrostatic pressure
- **C)** Raise peripheral resistance and BP
- **D)** Increase urine output
- **E)** Enhance filtration at capillaries

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q820: A patient with acute blood loss has increased catecholamine levels. Which of the following is least likely to occur as a result?

- **A)** Splenic contraction
- **B)** Decreased stroke volume
- **C)** Increased heart rate
- **D)** Increased systemic vascular resistance
- **E)** Venoconstriction

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q821: A 22-year-old female faints due to sudden blood loss. Her blood pressure is low, and she becomes tachycardic. Which reflex is initially responsible for increased sympathetic tone?

- **A)** Baroreceptor reflex
- **B)** Abdominal compression reflex
- **C)** Atrial stretch reflex
- **D)** Hering-Breuer reflex
- **E)** Diving reflex

**Correct Answer:** A

### Q822: A 40-year-old patient with blood loss has constriction of skin and splanchnic blood vessels. This redistribution helps:

- **A)** Raise intracranial pressure
- **B)** Reduce kidney perfusion
- **C)** Increase blood flow to skeletal muscles
- **D)** Preserve perfusion to vital organs
- **E)** Increase filtration in nephrons

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q823: A man experiences hemorrhage. Which factor most directly increases blood flow into circulation from the interstitial fluid?

- **A)** Increased capillary pressure
- **B)** Increased plasma protein concentration
- **C)** Decreased colloid osmotic pressure
- **D)** Decreased capillary hydrostatic pressure
- **E)** Increased interstitial hydrostatic pressure

### Q824: A trauma patient with acute blood loss has a drop in ABP. How does CNS ischemic response affect blood pressure?

- **A)** Inhibits the vasomotor center

- **C)** Causes bradycardia and vasodilation
- **D)** Stimulates the vasomotor center strongly
- **E)** Relaxes vascular smooth muscle

**Correct Answer:** D

### Q825: A young adult bleeds severely in an accident. Her BP is $70/40$ mmHg. Which physiological change is least likely to occur in the next few minutes?

- **A)** Increased venous return
- **B)** Increased urine output
- **C)** Generalized vasoconstriction
- **D)** Increased HR
- **E)** Increased catecholamines

**Correct Answer:** B

### Q826: Which of the following hormones is NOT typically secreted in response to acute hemorrhage?

- **A)** ADH
- **B)** Erythropoietin
- **C)** Insulin
- **D)** Aldosterone
