### Q827: Which of the following mechanisms helps restore plasma protein levels after hemorrhage?

- **C)** Increased liver synthesis using tissue and dietary proteins

- **E)** Secretion of renin

### Q828: A 45-year-old man experiences significant blood loss following a car accident. A few hours later, his blood pressure is still below normal. Which of the following mechanisms is most likely helping to maintain his vascular resistance?

- **A)** Increased secretion of insulin
- **B)** Secretion of ANP
- **C)** Secretion of aldosterone and ADH
- **D)** Activation of parasympathetic reflexes
- **E)** Secretion of glucagon

### Q829: A patient presents with signs of anemia four weeks after a hemorrhagic event. Which hormone would most likely be elevated to support recovery?

- **B)** Insulin

- **D)** ANP
- **E)** Renin

**Correct Answer:** C

### Q830: Following acute hemorrhage, a 50-year-old male experiences vasoconstriction and increased blood pressure within hours. Which long-term mechanism contributed most?

- **E)** Carotid body chemoreflex

### Q831: A patient suffers blood loss and, after 2 days, is retaining water and sodium. Which of the following changes contributed MOST to this condition?

- **A)** Inhibition of ADH and aldosterone
- **B)** Increased ANP secretion
- **C)** Reduced thirst response
- **D)** Activation of renin-angiotensin system and ADH release
- **E)** Increased baroreceptor activity

### Q832: A man with internal bleeding shows persistent low BP despite fluid therapy. Which mechanism most likely limits fluid loss through urine?

- **D)** Reverse stress relaxation
- **E)** Increased GFR

### Q833: A person experiences hemorrhage and has low atrial filling pressure. Which reflex is stimulated to help conserve blood volume?

### Q834: After hemorrhage, the decreased secretion of ANP leads to:

- **A)** Increased sodium excretion

- **C)** Sodium and water retention
- **D)** Decreased thirst

### Q835: A 30-year-old man is brought to the emergency department after a road traffic accident. He is pale, cold to the touch, with rapid, thready pulse and shallow breathing. His BP is 80/50 mmHg. What is the most likely underlying physiological cause of these findings?

### Q836: A 40-year-old man presents to the emergency room after suffering burns over 30% of his body. He develops hypotension, tachycardia, cold clammy skin, and reduced urine output. What is the most likely cause of his shock and its type?

### Q837: A 3-year-old child presents with severe diarrhea and vomiting for 2 days. On examination: sunken eyes, dry mouth, tachycardia, hypotension, and decreased skin turgor. What is the most likely type of shock?

- **D)** Septic shock

### Q838: A 32-year-old male is rushed to the ER after a motorcycle accident. He has multiple deep lacerations and is bleeding profusely. On examination, he is pale, has a rapid thready pulse, cold clammy skin, and hypotension. What is the most likely type of shock?

- **E)** Anaphylactic shock

### Q839: A 40-year-old man is admitted with third-degree burns over 45% of his body. Within hours, his blood pressure drops, heart rate increases, and skin becomes cold and clammy. Urine output is low. Which of the following is the type shock?

- **E)** Anaphylactic shock

### Q840: A 28-year-old construction worker is trapped under rubble for hours. After rescue, he is hypotensive, with rapid shallow breathing, pale cold skin, and reduced urine output. Labs show elevated creatinine and myoglobinuria. What is the most likely cause of his shock?

### Q841: A 6-year-old child presents with a history of 3 days of profuse diarrhea and vomiting. On exam: dry mucous membranes, sunken eyes, tachycardia, weak pulse, and cold extremities. Which of the following best explains his condition?

### Q842: A. 65-year-old male undergoes a lower abdominal surgery under spinal anesthesia. Shortly after, his blood pressure drops to 70/40 mmHg, with warm dry skin and low systemic vascular resistance. What is the most likely type shock in this case?

- **C)** Allergic shock
- **D)** cardiogenic shock
- **E)** Obstructive shock

### Q843: A healthy 22-year-old woman faints upon hearing the sudden death of a family member. On examination, she has hypotension, bradycardia, and warm extremities and was diagnosed as neurogenic shock. What is the most likely mechanism behind her symptoms?

### Q844: A 45-year-old man with known peanut allergy accidentally consumes peanut-containing food. Within minutes, he develops hypotension, wheezing, facial swelling, and warm flushed skin. What is the most immediate cause of his circulatory collapse?

- **E)** Cytokine release from macrophages

### Q845: A 60-year-old diabetic woman is admitted with fever, confusion, and low blood pressure. Her extremities are warm and flushed. Blood cultures grow E. coli. Which of the following best explains the vasodilation in this patient?

- **B)** Vagal overactivity
- **C)** Endotoxin-induced nitric oxide production

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q846: A 75-year-old nursing home resident presents with altered mental status, high fever, and a blood pressure of 85/50 mmHg. His skin is warm to touch, and lab results show elevated lactate and WBCs. What type of shock is most likely?

- **A)** Cardiogenic shock
- **B)** Obstructive shock
- **C)** Septic shock
- **D)** Hypovolemic shock
- **E)** Neurogenic shock

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q847: A 50-year-old woman develops hives, hypotension, and difficulty breathing 10 minutes after taking a sulfa antibiotic. She has warm skin and facial swelling. Which of the following contributes most to the fluid shift causing her hypotension?

- **A)** Bradykinin-induced bronchospasm
- **B)** Capillary permeability due to histamine
- **C)** Pulmonary embolism
- **D)** Myocardial depression due to hypoxia
- **E)** Reflex vasoconstriction

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q848: A 68-year-old male with a history of coronary artery disease presents with severe chest pain, shortness of breath, hypotension, cold extremities, and distended neck veins. Crackles are heard on lung auscultation. What is the most likely cause of his shock?

- **A)** Hypovolemic shock from internal bleeding
- **B)** Septic shock from pneumonia
- **C)** Cardiogenic shock from myocardial infarction
- **D)** Distributive shock from spinal injury
- **E)** Obstructive shock from pulmonary embolism

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q849: A 25-year-old male presents with fatigue, dyspnea, hypotension, distended neck veins, and. He had a viral illness a week ago.. What is the likely type of shock?

- **A)** Anaphylactic shock
- **B)** Neurogenic shock
- **C)** Cardiogenic shock due to acute myocarditis
- **D)** Hypovolemic shock due to dehydration
- **E)** Septic shock

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q850: A 75-year-old woman with a history of chronic heart failure presents with worsening dyspnea, orthopnea, hypotension, cold extremities, and visible neck vein distension. Auscultation reveals pulmonary rales. Which type of shock is she likely experiencing?

- **A)** Hypovolemic shock
- **B)** Cardiogenic shock
- **C)** Obstructive shock
- **D)** Septic shock
- **E)** Neurogenic shock

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q851: A 45-year-old man presents with hypotension, muffled heart sounds, and distended jugular veins. He had a recent penetrating chest injury. His pulse pressure is narrow, and ECG shows electrical alternans. What is the most likely cause of this patient's shock?

- **A)** Obstructive shock due to cardiac tamponade
- **B)** Cardiogenic shock due to arrhythmia
- **C)** Hypovolemic shock due to internal bleeding
- **D)** Septic shock
- **E)** Neurogenic shock

**Correct Answer:** A

---

### Q852: A 30-year-old man is brought to the emergency department after a motor vehicle accident. He is unconscious, hypotensive, tachycardic, and has distended neck veins. On exam, there is hyperresonance to percussion and absent breath sounds on the left. Trachea is deviated to the right. What is the likely cause of his shock?

- **A)** Cardiogenic shock from myocardial contusion
- **B)** Obstructive shock due to tension pneumothorax
- **C)** Hypovolemic shock from hemorrhage
- **D)** Neurogenic shock due to spinal injury
- **E)** Septic shock from aspiration pneumonia

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q853: Which of the following best characterizes the non-progressive (compensated) stage of hemorrhagic shock?

- **A)** Irreversible cellular damage despite fluid resuscitation
- **B)** Failure of all compensatory mechanisms
- **C)** Return of vital signs to normal without external therapy
- **D)** Progressive acidosis and cell membrane leakage
- **E)** Complete shutdown of renal function

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q854: What defines the irreversible (refractory) stage of circulatory shock?

- **A)** Increased cardiac output in response to fluids
- **B)** Recovery after volume replacement
- **C)** Resistance to vasoconstrictor drugs and persistent low COP
- **D)** Full compensation by sympathetic system
- **E)** Reversible ischemia of organs

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q855: A 30-year-old male is rushed to the emergency department after a motor vehicle accident. He is pale, restless, has a thready pulse, and systolic BP is 80 mmHg. After fluid resuscitation, his BP improves and mental status returns to normal. Which stage of hemorrhagic shock did he most likely experience?

- **A)** Irreversible shock
- **B)** Neurogenic shock
- **C)** Non-progressive shock
- **D)** Progressive shock
- **E)** Obstructive shock

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q856: A 55-year-old woman presents after prolonged gastrointestinal bleeding. Despite IV fluids and blood transfusion, her BP remains low, skin is cold, and urine output is negligible. Which stage of shock is she most likely in?

- **A)** Compensated shock
- **B)** Progressive shock
- **C)** Vasogenic shock
- **D)** Irreversible shock
- **E)** Distributive shock

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q857: A 64-year-old male with hemorrhagic shock is not responding to vasoconstrictor therapy. He has persistent hypotension and signs of cerebral hypoperfusion. Which mechanism is most responsible for vasomotor failure at this stage?

- **A)** Release of histamine and serotonin
- **B)** Accumulation of carbonic acid in tissues
- **C)** Ischemia of the vasomotor center in the brainstem
- **D)** Pooling of blood in peripheral capillaries
- **E)** Breakdown of lysosomes and cellular enzymes

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q858: A patient in progressive shock develops widespread edema and declining plasma volume despite fluid resuscitation. What is the most likely explanation?

- **A)** Vasomotor center failure
- **B)** Sludging of blood in microvessels
- **C)** Increased capillary permeability
- **D)** Cardiac arrhythmia
- **E)** Release of histamine from mast cells

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q859: A patient in late-stage hemorrhagic shock develops fever, hepatic failure, and a massive drop in systemic vascular resistance. Blood cultures grow gram-negative bacteria. What is the mechanism behind the worsening circulatory collapse?

- **A)** Bacterial toxins blocking sodium channels
- **B)** Generalized cellular degeneration
- **C)** Vasomotor center stimulation
- **D)** Entry of gut bacteria into portal circulation due to mucosal ischemia
- **E)** Pulmonary embolism

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q860: Which of the following contributes to cellular degeneration during irreversible shock?

- **A)** Enhanced protein synthesis
- **B)** Increased Na+/K+ pump activity
- **C)** Activation of mitochondrial ATP production
- **D)** Lysosomal rupture and enzyme release
- **E)** Decreased intracellular Ca2+

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q861: Which of the following substances is released from ischemic tissues and contributes to further circulatory deterioration in shock?

- **A)** Renin
- **B)** Epinephrine
- **C)** Histamine
- **D)** Aldosterone
- **E)** Cortisol

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q862: A 65-year-old man with long-standing hypertension presents with dyspnea on exertion, orthopnea, cold extremities, and cold, clammy skin. He has an S3 gallop and lung crackles. What type of heart failure does he most likely have?

- **A)** Right-sided heart failure
- **B)** Low-output, left-sided heart failure
- **C)** High-output heart failure
- **D)** Diastolic heart failure
- **E)** Cardiogenic shock

**Correct Answer:** B

---

### Q863: A patient with chronic mitral stenosis develops hepatomegaly, jugular venous distension, and peripheral edema but no pulmonary congestion. What type of failure is this?

- **A)** High-output HF
- **B)** Forward left-sided HF
- **C)** Right-sided backward HF
- **D)** Obstructive HF
- **E)** Acute left HF

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q864: A young woman with hyperthyroidism complains of fatigue and palpitations despite increased cardiac output (~8 L/min). She has warm extremities and no congestion. Diagnosis?

- **A)** Hypovolemic HF
- **B)** Low-output HF
- **C)** High-output HF
- **D)** Obstructive HF
- **E)** Acute left HF

**Correct Answer:** C

---

### Q865: An MI patient suddenly becomes dyspneic with frothy sputum, crackles, and elevated jugular venous pressure. What mechanism underlies his condition?

- **A)** Chronic systolic HF
- **B)** Acute left-sided HF with pulmonary edema
- **C)** Right-sided chronic HF
- **D)** High-output HF
- **E)** Diastolic HF

**Correct Answer:** E

---

### Q866: A patient with aortic stenosis has reduced stroke volume and forward flow but preserved EF. This is typical of:

- **A)** Systolic HF
- **B)** Diastolic HF
- **C)** High-output HF
- **D)** Low-output HF
- **E)** Obstructive HF

**Correct Answer:** D

---

### Q867: In CHF, what lab finding correlates with activation of the renin-angiotensin system?

- **A)** Low BNP
- **B)** Elevated ADH
- **C)** High aldosterone
- **D)** Increased ANP
- **E)** Reduced renin

**Correct Answer:** C
